<!-- definition-1: body_md -->
\label{defBLintro}
Let $V\subset X$ be relatively open and let $0<{\varepsilon}_0<1$. We say that a Radon
measure $\nu$ supported in $X$ belongs to $wBL_{{\varepsilon}_0}(\mu,V)$ if there exist
constants $0<\delta_0<1$ and $C_0\geq1$ such that
\begin{equation}\label{eqBLintro}
  \mu(2\Delta\cap E)<\delta_0\,\mu(2\Delta)
  \implies
  \nu(\Delta)\leq {\varepsilon}_0\,\nu(2\Delta\cap E)+C_0\,\nu(2\Delta\setminus E)
\end{equation}
for every Borel set $E\subset 2\Delta\subset V$.
<!-- end definition-1 -->
<!-- theoremletter-1: body_md -->
\label{theoAintro}
Let $\mu$ be a doubling Radon measure in ${\mathbb R}^d$ with doubling constant
$C_\mu$, let $X={\operatorname{supp}}\mu$, let $V\subset X$ be a bounded relatively open set
and let $K\subset V$ be compact with $\mu(K)>0$. Let $\nu$ be a Radon measure
supported in $X$ with
$$\nu\in wBL_{{\varepsilon}_0}(\mu,V),\qquad {\varepsilon}_0\leq{\varepsilon}_*,$$
where ${\varepsilon}_*>0$ depends only on $d$ and on $C_\mu$, and is made explicit in
\S\ref{secBL}.
Then $\nu|_V\ll\mu|_V$ and, writing $w=\frac{d\nu}{d\mu}$, there exists
$q_0>1$ depending on $d$, on $C_\mu$ and on the constants $\delta_0,C_0$ of
{(\ref{eqBLintro})}, such that for every $1<q\leq q_0$
$$\left(\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_K w^q\,d\mu\right)^{\frac1q}\leq C\,\frac{\nu(V)}{\mu(V)},$$
where $C$ depends in addition on $q$, on
$\dfrac{{\operatorname{diam}} K}{\operatorname{dist}(K,X\setminus V)}$ and on $\dfrac{\mu(V)}{\mu(K)}$.
<!-- end theoremletter-1 -->
<!-- corollaryletter-1: body_md -->
\label{coroBintro}
If $\mu$ is a doubling Radon measure in ${\mathbb R}^d$ and $V\subset X$ is relatively
open, then
$$wRH_1^U(\mu,V)=wRH_1^{QC}(\mu,V)=wRH_1^{BL}(\mu,V).$$
<!-- end corollaryletter-1 -->
<!-- theoremletter-2: body_md -->
\label{theoCintro}
Let $\gamma>1$, let $\mu$ be a doubling Radon measure in ${\mathbb R}^d$ with doubling
constant $C_\mu$, let $V\subset X$ be bounded and relatively open, and let
$K\subset V$ be compact with $\mu(K)>0$. Let $1<p<\infty$ and let
$\nu\ll\mu$, with density $w=\frac{d\nu}{d\mu}$, satisfy
$$\left(\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_{\Delta}w^p\, d\mu\right)^{\frac1p}
\leq C_{RH}\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_{\gamma\Delta}w\, d\mu
\qquad\text{for every restricted ball }\Delta\text{ with }\gamma\Delta\subset V .$$
Then there is ${\varepsilon}_0>0$, depending on $d$, $C_\mu$ and $p$ and on $\gamma$
and $C_{RH}$ only through the product $\gamma^{d}C_{RH}$, such that for every
$0<{\varepsilon}<{\varepsilon}_0$
$$\left(\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_{K}w^{p+{\varepsilon}}\, d\mu\right)^{\frac1{p+{\varepsilon}}}
\leq C\left(\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_{V}w^{p}\, d\mu\right)^{\frac1p},$$
with $C$ depending in addition on ${\varepsilon}$, on
$\frac{{\operatorname{diam}} K}{\operatorname{dist}(K,X\setminus V)}$ and on $\frac{\mu(V)}{\mu(K)}$.
<!-- end theoremletter-2 -->
<!-- lemmaletter-1: body_md -->
\label{lemDintro}
Let $\mu$ be a measure, let $V$ be measurable, let $p\geq1$ and let $f\geq0$
belong to $L^p(\mu|_V)$. Put $\sigma\coloneqq0$ if $p=1$ and $\sigma\coloneqq1$
if $p>1$, let $\rho>1$, $\alpha_j\coloneqq\rho^j\alpha_0$ and
$A^j\coloneqq\{x\in V:\, f(x)>\alpha_j\}$. If
$$\int_{A^{j}}f^p\, d\mu\leq C_1\,\alpha_{j+1}^{\,p-\sigma}
\int_{A^{j}}f^{\,\sigma}\, d\mu\quad\text{for every }j\geq0,
\qquad\text{with }\ \rho^{\,p-\sigma}>2C_1>2,$$
then there is $q_0>p$, given explicitly in terms of $C_1$, $\rho$ and $p$,
such that
$$\int_{V}f^q\, d\mu\leq C\,\alpha_0^{q-p}\int_{V}f^p\, d\mu
\qquad\text{for every }p<q\leq q_0 .$$
<!-- end lemmaletter-1 -->
<!-- remark-1: body_md -->
\label{remSkipping}
Passing from ${\mathcal D}$ to the subfamily $\bigcup_{k\in j{\mathbb Z}}{\mathcal D}_{k}$ of every
$j$-th generation again yields a grid, with $\rho_0$ replaced by $\rho_0^{j}$
and $A_0$ unchanged. We refer to this as \emph{skipping generations}. It lets
us assume that $\rho_0$ is as large as we please; we shall use it once, to
secure $\rho_0\geq 6$ in Remark \ref{rem3QNeighbors}.
<!-- end remark-1 -->
<!-- definition-2: body_md -->
\label{defNeighbors}
Let $\lambda\geq1$. Two cubes of the same generation $Q,R\in{\mathcal D}_k$ are
\emph{neighbours} if $\lambda B_Q\cap\lambda B_R\neq\varnothing$, and we write
$R\in{\mathcal N}(Q)$. The \emph{triple cube} of $Q$ is
$$3Q\coloneqq \bigcup_{R\in{\mathcal N}(Q)}R,$$
and we write $3Q\in3{\mathcal D}$. Since $Q\in{\mathcal N}(Q)$ and every $R\in{\mathcal N}(Q)$ satisfies
$R\subset B_R\subset B(z_Q,\lambda r_Q+2r_R)$, we have
\begin{equation}\label{eqTrivialInclusionDQP}
	\lambda B_Q\cap X\subset 3Q\subset 3\lambda B_Q .
\end{equation}
<!-- end definition-2 -->
<!-- remark-2: body_md -->
\label{rem3QNeighbors}
The neighbouring relation satisfies
\begin{enumerate}[N1.]
	\item \label{eqrem3QNeighbors0} $P\in{\mathcal N}(Q)\implies Q\subset 3P$.
\end{enumerate}
Moreover, we may fix $\lambda$ depending on $\rho_0$ so that, skipping
generations if necessary, the following also hold:
\begin{enumerate}[N1.]
	\setcounter{enumi}{1}
	\item \label{eqrem3QNeighbors2} $\left(\frac4{A_0}+1\right)B_Q\cap X\subset 3Q;$
	\item \label{eqrem3QNeighbors1} $\widehat Q\subset 3Q\subset 3\widehat Q;$
	\item \label{eqrem3QNeighbors3} $\lambda B_Q\subset\lambda B_{\widehat Q};$
	\item \label{eqrem3QNeighbors4} if $Q,P\in{\mathcal D}$ with $Q\cap P=\varnothing$
	and $z_Q\in3\lambda B_P$, then $3\lambda B_Q\subset 10\lambda^2A_0B_P;$ and
	\item \label{eqrem3QNeighbors5} if $P,R\in{\mathcal D}_k$ with $R\in{\mathcal N}(P)$, and
	$R\subset R'\in{\mathcal D}$ with $r_{R'}\geq\rho_0 r_P$, then $3P\subset 3R'$.
\end{enumerate}
From now on $A_0$, $\rho_0$ and $\lambda$ are dimensional constants, and
dependence on them is not displayed.
<!-- end remark-2 -->
<!-- proof-1: body_md -->
N\ref{eqrem3QNeighbors0} is immediate from the symmetry of Definition
\ref{defNeighbors}.

By Remark \ref{remSkipping} we may assume $\rho_0\geq 6$. Set
\begin{equation}\label{defLambda}
	\lambda\coloneqq 2\max\left\{(1-3\rho_0^{-1})^{-1},\,\rho_0\right\}\geq 6 .
\end{equation}
Property N\ref{eqrem3QNeighbors2} follows at once from
{(\ref{eqTrivialInclusionDQP})}.
                                                        

Let us prove N\ref{eqrem3QNeighbors1}. We first check that
$\lambda\geq(1-3\rho_0^{-1})^{-1}$ implies $3Q\subset3\widehat Q$. Let
$x\in3Q\cap R$ with $R,\widehat Q\in{\mathcal D}_{k}$. To see that $R$ and $\widehat Q$
are neighbours it suffices to show that $x\in\lambda B_{\widehat Q}$, and
indeed
$$|x-z_{\widehat Q}|\leq|x-z_Q|+|z_Q-z_{\widehat Q}|
\stackrel{\text{\color{grisclar}{(\ref{eqTrivialInclusionDQP})}}} <3\lambda r_Q+r_{\widehat Q}
=(3\lambda\rho_0^{-1}+1)r_{\widehat Q}
\stackrel{\text{\color{grisclar}{(\ref{defLambda})}}} \leq\lambda r_{\widehat Q}.$$
For the inclusion $\widehat Q\subset3Q$, assume $\lambda>\rho_0$ and let
$R\in{\mathcal D}_k$ with $\widehat R=\widehat Q$. Then
$$|z_Q-z_R|\leq{\operatorname{diam}}(B_{\widehat Q})=2\rho_0 r_Q=2\rho_0 r_R ,$$
so $\lambda B_Q\cap\lambda B_R\neq\varnothing$; note that the intersection
point need not belong to $X$.

The remaining properties are proved by the same techniques and are of similar difficulty, so we leave them to the reader.
<!-- end proof-1 -->
<!-- remark-3: body_md -->
\label{remAdjacentSystems}
The usual way of supplying a dilation on a closed set is different, and it is
worth recording what each device costs. Hyt\"onen and Kairema
\cite{HytonenKairema} produce finitely many \emph{adjacent} dyadic systems
${\mathcal D}^1,\dots,{\mathcal D}^K$ with the property that every ball $B$ is contained in some
cube of some ${\mathcal D}^t$ of comparable size; a condition on balls then becomes a
condition on ``some cube of some system''. This is the route taken in
\cite[\S4]{AndersonHytonenTapiola}, where the enlargement of a cube $Q$ is a
\emph{generalized dyadic parent}: a cube $Q^{*}$ of a coarser generation, with
$\ell(Q^{*})=\delta^{-2}\ell(Q)$, containing every cube of the generation of
$Q$ that meets $Q$. Since such a $Q^{*}$ is not unique, the resulting classes
are defined by a supremum over $Q$ and an infimum over the admissible $Q^{*}$.

Working with ${\mathcal D}\cup3{\mathcal D}$ instead has three consequences. First, everything
takes place in a single grid, and $3Q$ is determined by $Q$, so no infimum
over choices of enlargement enters the definitions. Second, the ratio between the diameters of
$3Q$ and $Q$ is governed by $\lambda$, which {(\ref{defLambda})} ties to $\rho_0$,
whereas a generalized dyadic parent is always two generations coarser. Third,
and this is what we actually need below, $3Q$ is a \emph{union of cubes of the
generation of $Q$}. The proof in \S\ref{secBL} uses exactly this:
the stopping condition of the Calder\'on--Zygmund decomposition is read off on
each $R\in{\mathcal N}(P)$ separately, and the estimate
$\nu(3P)\leq\nu(P)\bigl(1+C\mu(3P)/\mu(P)\bigr)$ is obtained by summing
$\mu(R)$ over $R\in{\mathcal N}(P)$. A single coarser cube does not decompose in this
way. The price is that $3Q$ is not itself a dyadic cube, so the family $3{\mathcal D}$
must be carried alongside ${\mathcal D}$ as a second layer of the basis, and its
localizations $3{\mathcal D}(Q_0)=\{3Q\cap Q_0:\, Q\in{\mathcal D}(Q_0)\}$ appear throughout.
Both features survive the localization, and this is what makes the second
layer usable: by D\ref{itemD2} the set $3Q\cap Q_0$ is again a union of cubes
of the generation of $Q$ whenever $Q\subset Q_0$, and by N\ref{eqrem3QNeighbors1} it still contains
the parent $\widehat Q$, as soon as $\ell(Q)<\ell(Q_0)$.
<!-- end remark-3 -->
<!-- lemma-1: body_md -->
\label{lemBesicovitchSelection}
Let $\mathcal F\subset{\mathcal D}$ be a family of pairwise disjoint cubes contained in
a bounded set. Then there is a subfamily $\mathcal G\subset\mathcal F$ with
$$\bigcup_{P\in\mathcal F}3P\subset\bigcup_{P\in\mathcal G}10\lambda^2A_0B_P
\qquad\text{and}\qquad\sum_{P\in\mathcal G}\chi_{3P}\leq C_{\mathrm B} ,$$
where $C_{\mathrm B}$ depends only on $d$.
<!-- end lemma-1 -->
<!-- proof-2: body_md -->
The centres $\{z_P\}_{P\in\mathcal F}$ form a bounded set, so the Besicovitch
covering theorem applied to the balls $\{3\lambda B_P\}_{P\in\mathcal F}$
yields $\mathcal G\subset\mathcal F$ with
$$\{z_P\}_{P\in\mathcal F}\subset\bigcup_{P\in\mathcal G}3\lambda B_P
\qquad\text{and}\qquad\sum_{P\in\mathcal G}\chi_{3\lambda B_P}\leq C_{\mathrm B} .$$
Given $P\in\mathcal F$, pick $P'\in\mathcal G$ with $z_P\in3\lambda B_{P'}$.
If $P'=P$ then $3P\subset3\lambda B_P\subset 10\lambda^2A_0B_P$ by
{(\ref{eqTrivialInclusionDQP})}; and if $P'\neq P$ the two cubes are disjoint, so
N\ref{eqrem3QNeighbors4} gives
$3P\subset3\lambda B_P\subset10\lambda^2A_0B_{P'}$. The second assertion
follows from $3P\subset3\lambda B_P$.
<!-- end proof-2 -->
<!-- lemma-2: body_md -->
\label{lemWhitney}
Let $X\subset{\mathbb R}^d$ be closed, let ${\mathcal D}$ be a dyadic grid associated to $X$
with $\rho_0$, $A_0$ and $\lambda\geq6$ as in Remark \ref{rem3QNeighbors}, and
let $\Omega\subset X$ be relatively open with $F\coloneqq X\setminus\Omega$
non-empty. Then there exist $\alpha=\alpha(\rho_0)>7$ and a collection
${\mathcal W}\subset{\mathcal D}$, a \emph{Whitney decomposition} of $\Omega$, such that:
\begin{enumerate}[Wh1.]
	\item \label{itemWhFull} $\Omega=\bigcup_{Q\in{\mathcal W}}Q$.
	\item \label{itemWhDisjoint} The cubes of ${\mathcal W}$ are pairwise disjoint, i.e.
	$\sum_{Q\in{\mathcal W}}\chi_Q=\chi_\Omega$.
	\item \label{itemWhRadius} $(\alpha\lambda-1)r_Q\leq\operatorname{dist}(Q,F)\leq
	\rho_0(\alpha\lambda+1)r_Q$ for every $Q\in{\mathcal W}$, and
	$3\widehat Q\subset \Omega$.
	\item \label{itemWhComparable} If $Q,R\in{\mathcal W}$ satisfy
	$3Q\cap3R\neq\varnothing$, then
	$\rho_0^{-1}\leq\frac{\ell(Q)}{\ell(R)}\leq\rho_0$.
	\item \label{itemWhOverlapping} The triple cubes have bounded overlap:
	$\sum_{Q\in{\mathcal W}}\chi_{3Q}\leq C_{\mathcal W}$, with $C_{\mathcal W}^{1/d}$ depending only on
	$\rho_0$, $A_0$ and $\lambda$.
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end lemma-2 -->
<!-- proof-3: body_md -->
Let ${\mathcal W}$ be the family of maximal dyadic cubes satisfying
\begin{equation}\label{eqStoppingTimeWhitney}
	\alpha\lambda r_Q\leq\operatorname{dist}(z_Q,F).
\end{equation}
Property Wh\ref{itemWhFull} follows from D\ref{itemD1} and D\ref{itemD4},
since for $x\in\Omega$ the cubes $Q_k(x)\in{\mathcal D}_k$ containing $x$ satisfy
$r_{Q_k(x)}=\frac{A_0}2\rho_0^{-k}\to0$ while
$\operatorname{dist}(z_{Q_k(x)},F)\to\operatorname{dist}(x,F)>0$ as $k\to\infty$. Property
Wh\ref{itemWhDisjoint} holds by construction and D\ref{itemD2}.

The lower bound in Wh\ref{itemWhRadius} follows from
{(\ref{eqStoppingTimeWhitney})} and the triangle inequality. For the upper bound,
maximality means that $\widehat Q$ fails {(\ref{eqStoppingTimeWhitney})}, so
\begin{equation}\label{eqDistCQAbove}
	\operatorname{dist}(z_Q,F)\leq\operatorname{dist}(z_{\widehat Q},F)+r_{\widehat Q}
	\stackrel{\text{\color{grisclar}{(\ref{eqStoppingTimeWhitney})}}} <(\alpha\lambda+1)r_{\widehat Q}
	=(\alpha\lambda+1)\rho_0 r_Q ,
\end{equation}
and the first part of Wh\ref{itemWhRadius} follows since
$\operatorname{dist}(Q,F)\leq\operatorname{dist}(z_Q,F)$. For the last part, assume
$\alpha\geq3\rho_0+1$ and let $x\in3\widehat Q$. Since
$3\widehat Q\subset3\lambda B_{\widehat Q}$ by {(\ref{eqTrivialInclusionDQP})} and
$z_Q\in Q\subset B_{\widehat Q}$,
$$\begin{aligned}
|x-z_Q|&\leq(3\lambda+1)r_{\widehat Q}=(3\lambda+1)\rho_0 r_Q
=\left(3\rho_0+\frac{\rho_0}\lambda\right)\lambda r_Q\\
&<\alpha\lambda r_Q\stackrel{\text{\color{grisclar}{(\ref{eqStoppingTimeWhitney})}}} \leq\operatorname{dist}(z_Q,F),
\end{aligned}$$
because $\lambda\geq2\rho_0$ by {(\ref{defLambda})}; hence $x\notin F$.

For Wh\ref{itemWhComparable}, assume $3R\cap3Q\neq\varnothing$ with
$r_R\leq r_Q$, and pick $x\in B(z_Q,3\lambda r_Q)\cap B(z_R,3\lambda r_R)$,
which exists by {(\ref{eqTrivialInclusionDQP})}. Then
$$\operatorname{dist}(z_R,F)\geq\operatorname{dist}(z_Q,F)-(3\lambda r_Q+3\lambda r_R)
\stackrel{\text{\color{grisclar}Wh\ref{itemWhRadius}}} \geq(\alpha\lambda-1-6\lambda)r_Q
\geq(\alpha-7)\lambda r_Q .$$
Combining with {(\ref{eqDistCQAbove})},
$$\frac{r_R}{r_Q}\geq\frac{(\alpha-7)\lambda}{\alpha\lambda+1}\rho_0^{-1}
\geq\frac{\alpha-7}{\alpha+1}\rho_0^{-1}>\rho_0^{-2}$$
provided $\alpha$ is large enough. Since $R$ and $Q$ are dyadic cubes with
$r_R\leq r_Q$, we have $\frac{\ell(R)}{\ell(Q)}=\frac{r_R}{r_Q}=\rho_0^{-j}$
for some $j\in{\mathbb N}$, and the estimate above forces $j\leq1$.

It remains to prove Wh\ref{itemWhOverlapping}. Let $x\in\Omega$ and let
$Q\in{\mathcal W}$ be a cube of maximal $r_Q$ with $x\in3Q$; such a cube exists by
Wh\ref{itemWhComparable}. Write
$D_P\coloneqq B\bigl(z_P,\tfrac12 r(\Delta_P)\bigr)$ for the Euclidean ball
with half the radius of the inner ball of $P$. For any $P\in{\mathcal W}$ with
$x\in3P$ we have, using {(\ref{eqTrivialInclusionDQP})} and
Wh\ref{itemWhComparable},
$$D_P\subset B\bigl(z_Q,3\lambda r_Q+3\lambda r_P+r(\Delta_P)\bigr)
\subset B\bigl(z_Q,(6\lambda+A_0^{-1})r_Q\bigr),$$
and moreover
$$r(\Delta_P)=A_0^{-1}r_P\stackrel{\text{\color{grisclar}Wh\ref{itemWhComparable}}} 
\geq A_0^{-1}\rho_0^{-1}r_Q .$$
The balls $\{D_P\}_{P\in{\mathcal D}_k}$ are pairwise disjoint: if $P,P'\in{\mathcal D}_k$ are
distinct, then $z_{P'}\in P'\subset X\setminus P$, so $z_{P'}\notin\Delta_P$
and hence $|z_P-z_{P'}|\geq r(\Delta_P)=r(\Delta_{P'})$. Comparing Lebesgue
measures we obtain
$$\begin{aligned}
\#\left\{P\in{\mathcal W}:\ 3P\cap3Q\neq\varnothing,\ r_P=\rho_0^{-1}r_Q\right\}
&\leq\frac{\bigl|B(z_Q,(6\lambda+A_0^{-1})r_Q)\bigr|}
{|B_1(0)|\left(\tfrac12 r(\Delta_P)\right)^d}\\
&\leq\bigl(2\rho_0(6\lambda A_0+1)\bigr)^d ,
\end{aligned}$$
and the same argument with $r_P=r_Q$ gives the analogous bound. By
Wh\ref{itemWhComparable} these are the only two possibilities, so
Wh\ref{itemWhOverlapping} holds with
$C_{\mathcal W}\leq(12\lambda A_0+2)^d(1+\rho_0)^d$.
<!-- end proof-3 -->
<!-- definition-3: body_md -->
\label{defDoubling}
A Radon measure $\mu$ in ${\mathbb R}^d$ is \emph{doubling} with constant $C_\mu$ if
$$\mu(B_{2r}(x))\leq C_\mu\,\mu(B_r(x))
\qquad\text{for every }x\in{\operatorname{supp}}\mu\text{ and every }r>0 .$$
<!-- end definition-3 -->
<!-- definition-4: body_md -->
\label{defDoublingConstant}
Let $\mu$ be a Radon measure in ${\mathbb R}^d$ and let $Q_0\in{\mathcal D}\cup3{\mathcal D}$. We say
that $\mu$ is \emph{${\mathcal D}$-doubling in $Q_0$} with constant $C_\mu$ if
$$\sup_{R\in{\mathcal N}(Q)\cap{\mathcal D}(Q_0)}\frac{\mu(3Q\cap Q_0)}{\mu(R)}\leq C_\mu
\qquad\text{for every }Q\in{\mathcal D}(Q_0).$$
<!-- end definition-4 -->
<!-- lemma-3: body_md -->
\label{lemDoublingNeighbors}
Let $\mu$ be a Radon measure in ${\mathbb R}^d$ and let $Q_0\in{\mathcal D}\cup3{\mathcal D}$. Then $\mu$
is ${\mathcal D}$-doubling in $Q_0$ with constant $C_\mu$ if and only if
\begin{equation}\label{eqDoublingComplement}
	\mu(3Q\cap Q_0\setminus R)\leq\left(1-\frac1{C_\mu}\right)\mu(3Q\cap Q_0)
\end{equation}
for every $Q\in{\mathcal D}(Q_0)$ and every $R\in{\mathcal N}(Q)\cap{\mathcal D}(Q_0)$.
In that case:
\begin{enumerate}[DN1.]
	\item \label{itemDN1} $\mu(3S)\leq C_\mu\mu(T)$ for every $S\in{\mathcal D}$ with
	$3S\subset Q_0$ and every $T\in{\mathcal N}(S)$;
	\item \label{itemDN2} $\mu(\widehat S)\leq C_\mu\mu(S)$ for every $S\in{\mathcal D}$
	with $\widehat S\subset Q_0$;
	\item \label{itemDN3} $\mu$ is ${\mathcal D}$-doubling in $Q_1$ for every
	$Q_1\in{\mathcal D}(Q_0)\cup3{\mathcal D}(Q_0)$.
\end{enumerate}
Moreover, if $\mu$ is doubling with constant $C_\mu$ in the sense of
Definition \ref{defDoubling}, then it is ${\mathcal D}$-doubling in every
$Q_0\in{\mathcal D}\cup3{\mathcal D}$ with constant $C_\mu^{N_0}$, where
$N_0=\lceil\log_2(5\lambda A_0)\rceil$.
<!-- end lemma-3 -->
<!-- proof-4: body_md -->
Since $R\in{\mathcal N}(Q)\cap{\mathcal D}(Q_0)$ implies $R\subset 3Q\cap Q_0$, and
$\mu(3Q\cap Q_0)<\infty$, we have
$\mu(3Q\cap Q_0\setminus R)=\mu(3Q\cap Q_0)-\mu(R)$. Hence
$\mu(3Q\cap Q_0)\leq C_\mu\mu(R)$ holds if and only if
$\mu(3Q\cap Q_0\setminus R)\leq(1-C_\mu^{-1})\mu(3Q\cap Q_0)$, and the
equivalence follows on taking the supremum over $R$.

                                                                        
                                                   
                                                                             
                                                                              
                                                               
                                                                 
                                                                        
                                                                      
                                                                        
Properties DN\ref{itemDN1}, DN\ref{itemDN2} and DN\ref{itemDN3} follow easily by applying the definitions and the tree structure of ${\mathcal D}$.



For the last assertion, let $R\in{\mathcal N}(Q)$. By Definition \ref{defNeighbors} we
have $|z_Q-z_R|\leq2\lambda r_Q$, so {(\ref{eqTrivialInclusionDQP})} gives
$3Q\cap Q_0\subset3\lambda B_Q\subset B(z_R,5\lambda r_Q)$, while
$\Delta_R\subset R$ has radius $A_0^{-1}r_Q$. Iterating Definition
\ref{defDoubling} $N_0$ times,
$$\mu(3Q\cap Q_0)\leq\mu\bigl(B(z_R,5\lambda r_Q)\bigr)
\leq C_\mu^{N_0}\mu(\Delta_R)\leq C_\mu^{N_0}\mu(R).\pushQED{\qed}\qedhere\popQED$$\renewcommand{}{}\vspace{-1em}
<!-- end proof-4 -->
<!-- lemma-4: body_md -->
\label{lemNeighborWhitney}
Let $\mu$ be a Radon measure in ${\mathbb R}^d$, let ${\mathcal D}$ be a dyadic grid associated
to $X={\operatorname{supp}}\mu$ with $\lambda\geq6$ as in Remark \ref{rem3QNeighbors}, and let
$U\subset X$ be relatively open with non-empty relative complement, such that
$\mu$ is ${\mathcal D}$-doubling in every $R\in{\mathcal D}\cup3{\mathcal D}$ with $R\subset U$. Let ${\mathcal W}$
be a Whitney decomposition of $U$. If $Q\in{\mathcal W}$ and $P\subsetneq Q$, then every
${\widetilde{Q}}\in{\mathcal W}$ with ${\widetilde{Q}}\cap3P\neq\varnothing$ satisfies
$$C_\mu^{-2}\mu(Q)\leq\mu({\widetilde{Q}})\leq C_\mu^{2}\mu(Q).$$
<!-- end lemma-4 -->
<!-- proof-5: body_md -->
We may assume ${\widetilde{Q}}\neq Q$. Iterating N\ref{eqrem3QNeighbors1} gives
$3P\subset3Q$. Let $x\in{\widetilde{Q}}\cap3P$; by Definition \ref{defNeighbors} there
is a cube $R\in{\mathcal N}(P)$ with $x\in R$. Since $3P\subset3Q$ and
${\widetilde{Q}}\subset3{\widetilde{Q}}$, we get $3{\widetilde{Q}}\cap3Q\neq\varnothing$, so
Wh\ref{itemWhComparable} shows that if $Q\in{\mathcal D}_{k_0}$ then
$${\widetilde{Q}}\in{\mathcal D}_{k_0-1}\cup{\mathcal D}_{k_0}\cup{\mathcal D}_{k_0+1}.$$
Since $P\subsetneq Q$ we deduce $R\subset{\widetilde{Q}}$, and
N\ref{eqrem3QNeighbors3} gives
$\lambda B_Q\cap\lambda B_{{\widetilde{Q}}}\supset\lambda B_P\cap\lambda B_R
\neq\varnothing$. In particular either $Q$ and ${\widetilde{Q}}$ are neighbours, or one
of them is a neighbour of the parent of the other.

By Lemma \ref{lemDoublingNeighbors} we have $\mu(3S)\leq C_\mu\mu(T)$ for
every $T\in{\mathcal N}(S)$ and $\mu(\widehat S)\leq C_\mu\mu(S)$, whenever
$3S\subset U$; this holds for all the cubes involved, since $\alpha>7$ in
Lemma \ref{lemWhitney}. Applying it to $S=Q$ and $S={\widetilde{Q}}$, and to their
parents when the generations differ, the lemma follows.
<!-- end proof-5 -->
<!-- lemma-5: body_md -->
\label{lemDyadicLebesgue}
Let $\mu$ be a Radon measure in ${\mathbb R}^d$, let $X={\operatorname{supp}}\mu$ and let ${\mathcal D}$ be a
dyadic grid associated to $X$. Writing $Q_k(x)$ for the cube of ${\mathcal D}_k$
containing $x$, every $f\in L^1_{{\rm loc}}(\mu)$ satisfies
$$\lim_{k\to\infty}\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_{Q_k(x)}f\, d\mu=f(x)
\qquad\text{for }\mu\text{-a.e. }x\in X .$$
<!-- end lemma-5 -->
<!-- proof-6: body_md -->
It suffices to argue on a fixed $Q_0\in{\mathcal D}$ with $\mu(Q_0)<\infty$ and
$f\in L^1(\mu|_{Q_0})$. The dyadic maximal operator
$M_{{\mathcal D}}f(x)\coloneqq \sup_{k}\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_{Q_k(x)}|f|\,d\mu$ is of weak type
$(1,1)$ with constant $1$, with no hypothesis on $\mu$: the set
$\{M_{\mathcal D} f>\lambda\}$ is the union of the maximal cubes $Q$ of ${\mathcal D}(Q_0)$ with
$\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_Q|f|\,d\mu>\lambda$, which are pairwise disjoint by D\ref{itemD2}, so
$$\mu(\{M_{\mathcal D} f>\lambda\})=\sum_j\mu(Q_j)
\leq\frac1\lambda\sum_j\int_{Q_j}|f|\,d\mu\leq\frac{\|f\|_{L^1(\mu)}}\lambda .$$
On the other hand, if $g$ is continuous then $\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_{Q_k(x)}g\,d\mu\to g(x)$
for every $x$, because $x\in Q_k(x)\subset B_{Q_k(x)}$ and
$r_{Q_k(x)}=\frac{A_0}2\rho_0^{-k}\to0$ by D\ref{itemD4}. Since $X$ is closed
in ${\mathbb R}^d$ and $\mu$ is Radon, continuous functions are dense in
$L^1(\mu|_{Q_0})$. The two facts combine in the usual way: given ${\varepsilon}>0$,
pick $g$ continuous with $\|f-g\|_{L^1(\mu|_{Q_0})}<{\varepsilon}$ and note that
$$\limsup_{k\to\infty}\left|\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_{Q_k(x)}f\, d\mu-f(x)\right|
\leq M_{\mathcal D}(f-g)(x)+|f-g|(x) ,$$
so that the set where the left-hand side exceeds $\lambda$ has measure at most
$4{\varepsilon}/\lambda$, by the weak type $(1,1)$ bound and Chebyshev's inequality.
Letting ${\varepsilon}\to0$ and then $\lambda\to0$ gives the conclusion.
<!-- end proof-6 -->
<!-- lemma-6: body_md -->
\label{lemCZDecomposition}
Let $\mu$ be a Radon measure in ${\mathbb R}^d$ and let $Q_0\in{\mathcal D}\cup3{\mathcal D}$ be such
that $\mu$ is ${\mathcal D}$-doubling in $Q_0$ with constant $C_\mu$. Let
$Q\in{\mathcal D}(Q_0)\cup3{\mathcal D}(Q_0)$, of generation $k_0$, let $f\in L^1(Q)$ be
non-negative and let
$$t\geq C_\mu^{-1}\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_Q f\, d\mu .$$
Then there exist a decomposition $Q=U_t\cup G_t\cup Z_t$ into disjoint
$\mu$-measurable sets and a family of pairwise disjoint cubes
$\mathcal{F}_t\subset\bigcup_{k\geq k_0}{\mathcal D}_k\cup\{Q\}$ such that
\begin{enumerate}[CZ1.]
	\item \label{itemCZU} $U_t=\bigcup_{R\in\mathcal{F}_t}R$, and every
	$R\in\mathcal{F}_t$ satisfies $t<\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_R f\, d\mu\leq C_\mu t$;
	\item \label{itemCZG} $f(x)\leq t$ for every $x\in G_t$;
	\item \label{itemCZZ} $\mu(Z_t)=0$.
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end lemma-6 -->
<!-- proof-7: body_md -->
Let $\mathcal{F}_t$ be the family of maximal cubes
$R\in\bigcup_{k\geq k_0}{\mathcal D}_k\cup\{Q\}$ with $R\subset Q$ and
$\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_R f\, d\mu>t$; let $U_t$ be as in CZ\ref{itemCZU}, let $Z_t$ be the
exceptional set of Lemma \ref{lemDyadicLebesgue} for $f$, and let
$G_t\coloneqq Q\setminus(U_t\cup Z_t)$.

If $t<\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_Q f\, d\mu$ then $\mathcal{F}_t=\{Q\}$, and CZ\ref{itemCZU} holds
because $t\geq C_\mu^{-1}\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_Q f\,d\mu$ by hypothesis; so we may assume
$Q\notin\mathcal{F}_t$. Then for $R\in\mathcal{F}_t$ either $\widehat R$ is an
eligible cube at which the stopping condition fails, or $R$ is one of the
building blocks of $Q$ when $Q\in3{\mathcal D}$. Using in the latter case the
convention $\widehat R=Q$, and Lemma \ref{lemDoublingNeighbors}, we get
$$\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_R f\, d\mu\leq C_\mu\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_{\widehat R}f\, d\mu\leq C_\mu t .$$
Conditions CZ\ref{itemCZG} and CZ\ref{itemCZZ} follow from Lemma
\ref{lemDyadicLebesgue}.
<!-- end proof-7 -->
<!-- lemma-7: body_md -->
\label{lemCZIteration}
In the setting of Lemma \ref{lemCZDecomposition}, let $0<\tau<1$ and
$\alpha_0\geq C_\mu^{-1}\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_Q f\, d\mu$, and put
$\alpha_j\coloneqq \left[C_\mu\tau^{-1}\right]^{j}\alpha_0$ for $j\geq1$. Then
the decompositions $Q=U^j\cup G^j\cup Z^j$ given by Lemma
\ref{lemCZDecomposition} at the levels $\alpha_j$, with
$U^j=\bigcup_{R\in\mathcal{F}^j}R$, satisfy:
\begin{enumerate}[CZ1.]
	\setcounter{enumi}{3}
	\item \label{itemCZIhat} if $R\in\mathcal{F}^j$ then $R\subset\hat R$ for
	some $\hat R\in\mathcal{F}^{j-1}$;
	\item \label{itemCZLayers} $\mu(U^{j+1})\leq\tau\mu(U^j)\leq\cdots\leq
	\tau^{j+1}\mu(U^0)\xrightarrow{j\to\infty}0$;
	\item \label{itemCZLayersByCubes} if $R\in\mathcal{F}^j$ then
	$\mu(R\cap U^{j+1})<\tau\mu(R)$.
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end lemma-7 -->
<!-- proof-8: body_md -->
For CZ\ref{itemCZIhat}, note that $R\in\mathcal{F}^{j+1}$ satisfies
$\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_R f\, d\mu>\alpha_{j+1}>\alpha_j$, so $R$ is contained in some maximal
cube with that property, possibly $R$ itself.

For CZ\ref{itemCZLayersByCubes}, let $R\in\mathcal{F}^j$. Then
$$
\begin{aligned}
	C_\mu\alpha_j
	&\stackrel{\text{\color{grisclar}CZ\ref{itemCZU}}} \geq\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_R f\, d\mu
	\geq\frac1{\mu(R)}\int_{R\cap U^{j+1}}f\, d\mu
	\stackrel{\text{\color{grisclar}CZ\ref{itemCZIhat}}} =\frac1{\mu(R)}
	\sum_{R'\in\mathcal{F}^{j+1}:\,R'\subset R}\mu(R')\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_{R'}f\, d\mu\\
	&\stackrel{\text{\color{grisclar}CZ\ref{itemCZU}}} >\frac{\alpha_{j+1}}{\mu(R)}\mu(R\cap U^{j+1}),
\end{aligned}
$$
and CZ\ref{itemCZLayersByCubes} follows since
$\alpha_{j+1}=C_\mu\tau^{-1}\alpha_j$. Finally CZ\ref{itemCZLayers} is
obtained by summing CZ\ref{itemCZLayersByCubes} over $R\in\mathcal{F}^j$ and
using CZ\ref{itemCZIhat}.
<!-- end proof-8 -->
<!-- definition-5: body_md -->
\label{defBasis}
A \emph{basis} is either the family of restricted balls of $X$ or the family
${\mathcal D}\cup3{\mathcal D}$. In the first case $V\subset X$ denotes a relatively open set and
$${\mathcal B}_V\coloneqq \{\Delta\subset V:\, \Delta\text{ a restricted ball}\};$$
in the second, $V$ is a cube $Q_0\in{\mathcal D}\cup3{\mathcal D}$ and
${\mathcal B}_{Q_0}\coloneqq {\mathcal D}(Q_0)\cup3{\mathcal D}(Q_0)$. Statements made for ${\mathcal B}_V$ with no
further qualification hold in both cases, and when $V=X$ we drop it from the
notation. Throughout the text, ${\mathcal B}_{\mathcal{D}}$ will refer to the dyadic basis $\mathcal{D}\cup3\mathcal{D}$.
<!-- end definition-5 -->
<!-- definition-6: body_md -->
\label{defStrongClasses}
Let $\mu$ be a Radon measure in ${\mathbb R}^d$, let $X={\operatorname{supp}}\mu$, let ${\mathcal B}_V$ be as in
Definition \ref{defBasis}, let $w\in L^1_{{\rm loc}}(\mu)$ and let $1<p<\infty$.
\begin{itemize}
	\item $w\in A_p(\mu,{\mathcal B}_V)$ if $w^{\frac1{1-p}}\in L^1_{{\rm loc}}(\mu)$ and
	$$\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_{B}w\, d\mu\left(\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_{B}w^{\frac1{1-p}}\, d\mu\right)^{p-1}
	\leq C\qquad\text{for every }B\in{\mathcal B}_V .$$
	\item $w\in RH_p(\mu,{\mathcal B}_V)$ if $w\in L^p_{{\rm loc}}(\mu)$, $w>0$ $\mu$-a.e.\
	and
	\begin{equation}\label{eqStrongRH}
		\left(\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_{B}w^p\, d\mu\right)^{\frac1p}\leq C\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_{B}w\, d\mu
		\qquad\text{for every }B\in{\mathcal B}_V .
	\end{equation}
\end{itemize}
The least admissible constants are denoted $[w]_{A_p(\mu,{\mathcal B}_V)}$ and
$[w]_{RH_p(\mu,{\mathcal B}_V)}$, and we set
$$A_\infty^{U}(\mu,{\mathcal B}_V)\coloneqq \bigcup_{1<p<\infty}A_p(\mu,{\mathcal B}_V),
\qquad
RH_1^{U}(\mu,{\mathcal B}_V)\coloneqq \bigcup_{1<p<\infty}RH_p(\mu,{\mathcal B}_V),$$
the superscript standing for \emph{union}.
<!-- end definition-6 -->
<!-- definition-7: body_md -->
\label{defQC}
In the setting of Definition \ref{defStrongClasses}, let $\nu$ be a Radon
measure supported in $X$ and let $0<\delta,{\varepsilon}<1$. We say that
$\nu\in QC_{\delta,{\varepsilon}}(\mu,{\mathcal B}_V)$ if
\begin{equation}\label{eqDefQC}
	\mu(E)<\delta\,\mu(B)\implies\nu(E)\leq{\varepsilon}\,\nu(B)
	\qquad\text{for every Borel }E\subset B\in{\mathcal B}_V ,
\end{equation}
and we put $QC(\mu,{\mathcal B}_V)\coloneqq
\bigcup_{0<\delta,{\varepsilon}<1}QC_{\delta,{\varepsilon}}(\mu,{\mathcal B}_V)$ and
$$\begin{aligned}
A_\infty^{QC}(\mu,{\mathcal B}_V)&\coloneqq \bigcap_{0<\delta<1}\bigcup_{0<{\varepsilon}<1}
QC_{\delta,{\varepsilon}}(\mu,{\mathcal B}_V),\\
RH_1^{QC}(\mu,{\mathcal B}_V)&\coloneqq \bigcap_{0<{\varepsilon}<1}\bigcup_{0<\delta<1}
QC_{\delta,{\varepsilon}}(\mu,{\mathcal B}_V).
\end{aligned}$$
Here $QC$ stands for \emph{quantitative (mutual absolute) continuity}, for the
reason given in Remark \ref{remWMutual}.
<!-- end definition-7 -->
<!-- remark-4: body_md -->
\label{remWMutual}
Condition {(\ref{eqDefQC})} is a quantitative form of mutual absolute continuity.
First, it forces $\nu|_V\ll\mu|_V$. Indeed, let $E\subset V$ be Borel with
$\nu(E)>0$; applying the Lebesgue differentiation theorem for $\nu$ to
$\chi_E$ --- which is available for restricted balls because $\nu$ is a Radon
measure in ${\mathbb R}^d$, by the Besicovitch covering theorem, and for
${\mathcal D}\cup3{\mathcal D}$ by Lemma \ref{lemDyadicLebesgue} --- we find $B\in{\mathcal B}_V$,
arbitrarily small, with $\nu(E\cap B)>{\varepsilon}\,\nu(B)$; then {(\ref{eqDefQC})} gives
$\mu(E)\geq\mu(E\cap B)\geq\delta\,\mu(B)>0$.

Second, replacing $E$ by $B\setminus E$ shows that {(\ref{eqDefQC})} is equivalent
to
\begin{equation}\label{eqDefQCbis}
	\mu(E)>(1-\delta)\,\mu(B)\implies\nu(E)\geq(1-{\varepsilon})\,\nu(B)
\end{equation}
for every Borel $E\subset B\in{\mathcal B}_V$,
and hence, taking contrapositives, to
$\nu(E)<(1-{\varepsilon})\nu(B)\implies\mu(E)\leq(1-\delta)\mu(B)$. In particular
${\operatorname{supp}}\mu={\operatorname{supp}}\nu$ and $\mu|_V\ll\nu|_V$ as well.

We also write $\nu\in RH_\infty(\mu,{\mathcal B}_V)$ when $\sup_Bw\leq C\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_Bw\, d\mu$
for every $B\in{\mathcal B}_V$; this is the strongest of the conditions below, and
$RH_\infty\subset RH_p$ for every $p$.

Consequently $d\nu=w\, d\mu$ with $0<w<\infty$ both $\mu$- and $\nu$-a.e.,
with $w\in L^1_{{\rm loc}}(\mu|_V)$ and $w^{-1}\in L^1_{{\rm loc}}(\nu|_V)$, and we
write indifferently $\nu\in QC_{\delta,{\varepsilon}}(\mu,{\mathcal B}_V)$ or
$w\in QC_{\delta,{\varepsilon}}(\mu,{\mathcal B}_V)$; the same convention applies to all the
classes above.
<!-- end remark-4 -->
<!-- lemma-8: body_md -->
\label{lemTrivialImplications}
Let $\mu$ be a Radon measure in ${\mathbb R}^d$, let ${\mathcal B}_V$ be as in Definition
\ref{defBasis} and let $\nu$ be a Radon measure supported in $X$. Then
\begin{equation}\label{eqTrivialImplications}
	A_\infty^{U}\subset A_\infty^{QC}\subset QC
	\supset RH_1^{QC}\supset RH_1^{U},
\end{equation}
all classes taken with respect to $(\mu,{\mathcal B}_V)$, and
\begin{equation}\label{eqDualityWeights}
\begin{aligned}
	\nu\in A_p(\mu,{\mathcal B}_V)&\iff\mu\in RH_{p'}(\nu,{\mathcal B}_V),\\
	\nu\in QC_{\delta,{\varepsilon}}(\mu,{\mathcal B}_V)&\iff
	\mu\in QC_{1-{\varepsilon},1-\delta}(\nu,{\mathcal B}_V),\\
	\nu\in A_\infty^{QC}(\mu,{\mathcal B}_V)&\iff\mu\in RH_1^{QC}(\nu,{\mathcal B}_V).
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}
Moreover, if $w\in RH_p(\mu,{\mathcal B}_V)$ with constant $C_1$, then
$\nu\in QC_{\delta,{\varepsilon}}(\mu,{\mathcal B}_V)$ for every $0<{\varepsilon}<1$, with
$\delta\coloneqq \left(C_1^{-1}{\varepsilon}\right)^{p'}$.
<!-- end lemma-8 -->
<!-- proof-9: body_md -->
The first line of {(\ref{eqDualityWeights})} is
\cite[Rem.~2.3]{DuoandikoetxeaMartinReyesOmbrosi}, valid over any basis, with
$[w]_{A_p(\mu,{\mathcal B}_V)}=[w^{-1}]_{RH_{p'}(\nu,{\mathcal B}_V)}^{p}$; we do not reprove
it. The second line is the equivalence between {(\ref{eqDefQC})} and
{(\ref{eqDefQCbis})} established in Remark \ref{remWMutual}, read with the roles of
$\mu$ and $\nu$ interchanged.

The third line follows from the second by inspecting the quantifiers. Writing
$\delta'=1-{\varepsilon}$ and ${\varepsilon}'=1-\delta$, which is an involution of
$(0,1)\times(0,1)$, we get
$$\nu\in\bigcap_{\delta}\bigcup_{{\varepsilon}}QC_{\delta,{\varepsilon}}(\mu,{\mathcal B}_V)
\iff\mu\in\bigcap_{{\varepsilon}'}\bigcup_{\delta'}QC_{\delta',{\varepsilon}'}(\nu,{\mathcal B}_V),$$
because the involution exchanges the two coordinates: a $\delta$ quantified
first becomes an ${\varepsilon}'$ quantified first, and an ${\varepsilon}$ quantified second
becomes a $\delta'$ quantified second. 
                      
                                                                              

For the last assertion, let $E\subset B\in{\mathcal B}_V$ be Borel. By H\"older's
inequality and {(\ref{eqStrongRH})},
$$\frac{\nu(E)}{\nu(B)}=\frac1{\nu(B)}\int_{E}w\, d\mu
\leq\frac{\mu(B)}{\nu(B)}\left(\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_{B}w^{p}\, d\mu\right)^{\frac1p}
\left(\frac{\mu(E)}{\mu(B)}\right)^{\frac1{p'}}
\leq C_1\left(\frac{\mu(E)}{\mu(B)}\right)^{\frac1{p'}},$$
so $\mu(E)<\delta\mu(B)$ with $\delta=(C_1^{-1}{\varepsilon})^{p'}$ gives
$\nu(E)\leq{\varepsilon}\,\nu(B)$.

Finally we obtain {(\ref{eqTrivialImplications})}. The last assertion just proved
gives $RH_1^{U}\subset RH_1^{QC}$, and the inclusions
$A_\infty^{QC}\subset QC$ and $RH_1^{QC}\subset QC$ are immediate from the
definitions. For the remaining one, $\nu\in A_\infty^{U}(\mu,{\mathcal B}_V)$ means
$\nu\in A_p(\mu,{\mathcal B}_V)$ for some $p$, hence $\mu\in RH_{p'}(\nu,{\mathcal B}_V)\subset
RH_1^{QC}(\nu,{\mathcal B}_V)$ by the first and last lines of the proof, hence
$\nu\in A_\infty^{QC}(\mu,{\mathcal B}_V)$ by the third line of
{(\ref{eqDualityWeights})}.
<!-- end proof-9 -->
<!-- remark-5: body_md -->
\label{remDMOwarning}
Outside the doubling setting nothing of the sort survives. Duoandikoetxea,
Mart\'in-Reyes and Ombrosi consider eight conditions of this kind over a
general basis, including $A_\infty^{U}$, $RH_1^{U}$ and $QC$, and show that for suitable sparse bases no two of them coincide
\cite[Cor.~6.2]{DuoandikoetxeaMartinReyesOmbrosi}. Related negative and positive results for particular bases are in
\cite{MudarraPerfekt} and in
\cite[Rem.~3.4]{HagelsteinLuqueParissis}.
<!-- end remark-5 -->
<!-- theorem-1: body_md -->
\label{theoEqualityRH}
If $\mu$ is ${\mathcal D}$-doubling in $Q_0\in{\mathcal D}\cup3{\mathcal D}$, then
\begin{equation}\label{eqEqualityBpDyadic}
	RH_1^{U}(\mu,{\mathcal B}_{Q_0})=RH_1^{QC}(\mu,{\mathcal B}_{Q_0})=QC(\mu,{\mathcal B}_{Q_0}),
\end{equation}
and we write $RH(\mu,{\mathcal B}_{Q_0})$ for this class.
<!-- end theorem-1 -->
<!-- theorem-2: body_md -->
\label{theoQCinfty}
Let $\mu$ and $\nu$ be Radon measures in ${\mathbb R}^d$ with
${\operatorname{supp}}\nu\subset{\operatorname{supp}}\mu$, let $Q_0\in{\mathcal D}\cup3{\mathcal D}$, let $\mu$ be
${\mathcal D}$-doubling in $Q_0$ with constant $C_\mu$, and let $0<\delta_0,{\varepsilon}_0<1$
be such that $\nu\in QC_{\delta_0,{\varepsilon}_0}(\mu,{\mathcal B}_{Q_0})$. Then the density
$w=\frac{d\nu}{d\mu}$ lies in $RH_p(\mu,{\mathcal B}_{Q_0})$ whenever
\begin{equation}\label{eqPZero}
	1<p<p_0\coloneqq 1+\frac{\log(1/{\varepsilon}_0)}{\log(C_\mu/\delta_0)} ,
\end{equation}
and, writing $p-1=\theta\,(p_0-1)$ with $0<\theta<1$,
$$\left(\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_{Q}w^p\, d\mu\right)^{\frac1p}
\leq\left(1+\frac{{\varepsilon}_0^{-\theta}}{1-{\varepsilon}_0^{1-\theta}}\right)^{\frac1p}
\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_{Q}w\, d\mu\qquad\text{for every }Q\in{\mathcal B}_{Q_0} .$$
<!-- end theorem-2 -->
<!-- proof-10: body_md -->
Fix $Q\in{\mathcal B}_{Q_0}={\mathcal D}(Q_0)\cup3{\mathcal D}(Q_0)$ and apply Lemma
\ref{lemCZIteration} with $f=w$, with
$\alpha_0=\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_{Q}w\, d\mu=\frac{\nu(Q)}{\mu(Q)}$ and with
$\tau=\delta_0$, so that $\alpha_j=\left(C_\mu\delta_0^{-1}\right)^{j}\alpha_0$.
For $R\in\mathcal F^j$, property CZ\ref{itemCZLayersByCubes} reads
$\mu(R\cap U^{j+1})<\delta_0\,\mu(R)$, so {(\ref{eqDefQC})} applied to
$E=R\cap U^{j+1}\subset R\in{\mathcal B}_{Q_0}$ gives
$\nu(R\cap U^{j+1})\leq{\varepsilon}_0\,\nu(R)$; summing over the disjoint family
$\mathcal F^j$,
\begin{equation}\label{eqNuLayers}
	\nu(U^{j+1})\leq{\varepsilon}_0\,\nu(U^{j})\leq\cdots\leq{\varepsilon}_0^{\,j+1}\nu(U^{0}) .
\end{equation}
By CZ\ref{itemCZZ} the sets $Q\setminus U^0$ and $U^j\setminus U^{j+1}$,
$j\geq0$, cover $Q$ up to a set that is null for both measures, and on each of
them CZ\ref{itemCZG} bounds $w$ by $\alpha_0$ and by $\alpha_{j+1}$
respectively. Hence
$$\begin{aligned}
\int_{Q}w^p\, d\mu
&=\int_{Q\setminus U^{0}}w^{p-1}w\, d\mu
+\sum_{j\geq0}\int_{U^{j}\setminus U^{j+1}}w^{p-1}w\, d\mu\\
&\leq\alpha_0^{p-1}\nu(Q\setminus U^{0})
+\sum_{j\geq0}\alpha_{j+1}^{p-1}\,\nu(U^{j})\\
&\stackrel{\text{\color{grisclar}{(\ref{eqNuLayers})}}} \leq\alpha_0^{p-1}
\left(1+\sum_{j\geq0}\left(C_\mu\delta_0^{-1}\right)^{(j+1)(p-1)}
{\varepsilon}_0^{\,j}\right)\nu(Q) .
\end{aligned}$$
Now $p-1=\theta(p_0-1)$ is exactly
$\left(C_\mu\delta_0^{-1}\right)^{p-1}={\varepsilon}_0^{-\theta}$, so the series is
geometric of ratio ${\varepsilon}_0^{1-\theta}<1$ and sums to
${\varepsilon}_0^{-\theta}\left(1-{\varepsilon}_0^{1-\theta}\right)^{-1}$. Since
$\alpha_0=\frac{\nu(Q)}{\mu(Q)}$, dividing by $\mu(Q)$ gives the stated
inequality.
<!-- end proof-10 -->
<!-- lemma-9: body_md -->
\label{lemBothDoubling}
Let $\mu$ be a Radon measure in ${\mathbb R}^d$, ${\mathcal D}$-doubling in
$Q_0\in{\mathcal D}\cup3{\mathcal D}$ with constant $C_\mu$, and let
$\nu\in QC_{\delta_0,{\varepsilon}_0}(\mu,{\mathcal B}_{Q_0})$ with
$1-C_\mu^{-1}<\delta_0<1$ and $0<{\varepsilon}_0<1$. Then $\nu$ is ${\mathcal D}$-doubling in
$Q_0$, with constant $(1-{\varepsilon}_0)^{-1}$. In particular
$$A_\infty^{QC,d}(\mu,{\mathcal B}_{Q_0})=A_\infty^{QC}(\mu,{\mathcal B}_{Q_0}).$$
<!-- end lemma-9 -->
<!-- proof-11: body_md -->
By Lemma \ref{lemDoublingNeighbors}, $\mu$ being ${\mathcal D}$-doubling in $Q_0$ means
that $\mu(3Q\cap Q_0\setminus R)\leq(1-C_\mu^{-1})\mu(3Q\cap Q_0)$ for every
$Q\in{\mathcal D}(Q_0)$ and every $R\in{\mathcal N}(Q)\cap{\mathcal D}(Q_0)$. Since
$\delta_0>1-C_\mu^{-1}$, condition {(\ref{eqDefQC})} applied to
$E=3Q\cap Q_0\setminus R$ inside $B=3Q\cap Q_0\in{\mathcal B}_{Q_0}$ gives
$$\nu(3Q\cap Q_0\setminus R)\leq{\varepsilon}_0\,\nu(3Q\cap Q_0),$$
that is, $\nu(3Q\cap Q_0)\leq(1-{\varepsilon}_0)^{-1}\nu(R)$. The last assertion follows
by choosing any $\delta_0\in\left(1-C_\mu^{-1},1\right)$ in the definition of
$A_\infty^{QC}$.
<!-- end proof-11 -->
<!-- theorem-3: body_md -->
\label{theoEqualityA}
If $\mu$ is ${\mathcal D}$-doubling in $Q_0\in{\mathcal D}\cup3{\mathcal D}$, then
\begin{equation}\label{eqBothDoubling}
	A_\infty^{U}(\mu,{\mathcal B}_{Q_0})=A_\infty^{QC}(\mu,{\mathcal B}_{Q_0})
	=QC^{d}(\mu,{\mathcal B}_{Q_0}),
\end{equation}
and we write $A_\infty(\mu,{\mathcal B}_{Q_0})$ for this class.
<!-- end theorem-3 -->
<!-- example-1: body_md -->
\label{exStaircase}
Let $\mu$ be the Lebesgue measure in ${\mathbb R}$, let ${\mathcal D}$ be the standard dyadic
grid, let $Q_0=[0,1)$ and put
$$I_0\coloneqq \left[0,\tfrac12\right),\quad
I_k\coloneqq \left[1-2^{-k},1-2^{-k-1}\right)\ \ (k\geq1),\quad
w\coloneqq \sum_{k\geq0}2^{-k^{2}}\chi_{I_k} .$$
Then $w>0$ and $\nu=w\mu$ lies in $QC(\mu,{\mathcal B}_{Q_0})$, hence in
$RH_1^{U}(\mu,{\mathcal B}_{Q_0})$ by Theorem \ref{theoEqualityRH}, but $\nu$ is not
${\mathcal D}$-doubling in $Q_0$, so $\nu\notin A_\infty^{U}(\mu,{\mathcal B}_{Q_0})$ by Theorem
\ref{theoEqualityA}. Let us check these two facts.

Write $J_m\coloneqq \left[1-2^{-m},1\right)=\bigcup_{j\geq m}I_j$, so that
$J_m\in{\mathcal D}_m$ has children $I_m$ and $J_{m+1}$, and the ancestors of $I_k$ are
exactly $J_k,J_{k-1},\dots,J_0$. Consequently every $Q\in{\mathcal D}(Q_0)$ either is
contained in some $I_k$, where $w$ is constant, or equals some $J_m$, where
$$\sup_{J_m}w=2^{-m^{2}}\qquad\text{and}\qquad
\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_{J_m}w\, d\mu\geq2^{m}\,2^{-m^{2}}\left|I_m\right|=2^{-m^{2}-1} ;$$
in both cases $\sup_Qw\leq2\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_Qw\, d\mu$. If now
$B=3Q\cap Q_0\in3{\mathcal D}(Q_0)$ with $Q\in{\mathcal D}_m$, then $B$ is a union of at most
$N=N(\lambda)$ cubes of ${\mathcal D}_m$ by Remark \ref{remAdjacentSystems}, and
applying the previous bound to the one on which $w$ is largest gives
$\sup_Bw\leq2N\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_Bw\, d\mu$. So $w\leq2N\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_Bw\, d\mu$ on every
$B\in{\mathcal B}_{Q_0}$, whence
$$\mu(E)<\delta\,\mu(B)\implies\nu(E)\leq2N\delta\,\nu(B)
\qquad\text{for every Borel }E\subset B\in{\mathcal B}_{Q_0},$$
which is {(\ref{eqDefQC})} with ${\varepsilon}=2N\delta$, admissible for $\delta<(2N)^{-1}$.

On the other hand $\nu(J_m)=\sum_{j\geq m}2^{-j^{2}-j-1}\leq2^{-m^{2}-m}$,
while $\widehat{J_m}=J_{m-1}$ contains $I_{m-1}$, so
$$\frac{\nu(\widehat{J_m})}{\nu(J_m)}
\geq\frac{2^{-(m-1)^{2}}\left|I_{m-1}\right|}{2^{-m^{2}-m}}
=2^{\,2m-2}\xrightarrow[m\to\infty]{}\infty ,$$
and DN\ref{itemDN2} rules out
${\mathcal D}$-doubling. That $w$ lies in no $A_p$ can also be seen directly: since $w$
decays superexponentially along the $I_j$, the average
$\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_{J_m}w^{-\frac1{p-1}}\, d\mu$ is infinite for every $p>1$.
<!-- end example-1 -->
<!-- remark-6: body_md -->
The mechanism of the preceding example is the one isolated in Remark \ref{remNoDyadicAnalogue} below:
here {(\ref{eqDefQC})} holds only for small $\delta$, and a small $\delta$ cannot
see the single large step from $J_m$ to its parent.

The construction can be replicated for an arbitrary Radon measure $\mu$, as long as the measure of a cube is not concentrated on a particular child (to be precise, we need $\mu(Q_m)\leq (1-1/m) \mu(\hat Q_m)$). It may fail, for instance, when $\mu$ has point masses.
<!-- end remark-6 -->
<!-- remark-7: body_md -->
\label{remDimensionalConstant}
Property D\ref{itemD4} attaches to each $Q\in{\mathcal D}$ restricted balls
$\Delta_Q\subset Q\subset B_Q\cap X$ with $r(B_Q)=A_0\,r(\Delta_Q)$. A routine
verification from N\ref{eqrem3QNeighbors2} produces the same for every
$Q\in3{\mathcal D}$ and every $Q\in3{\mathcal D}(Q_0)$, at the cost of \emph{increasing} $A_0$
to a dimensional constant ${\widetilde{A}}_0$ with
${\widetilde{A}}_0^{-1}B_Q\cap X\subset\Delta_Q$. From now on $A_0$ denotes the enlarged
constant.
<!-- end remark-7 -->
<!-- lemma-10: body_md -->
\label{lemApDyadicornot}
Let $\mu$ be a doubling Radon measure in ${\mathbb R}^d$, let $Q_0\in{\mathcal D}\cup3{\mathcal D}$ and
let $1<p<\infty$. Then
$$A_p(\mu,V_{Q_0})\subset A_p(\mu,{\mathcal B}_{Q_0})\subset A_p(\mu,\mathring Q_0),$$
and the same with $RH_p$ in place of $A_p$. In particular
$A_p(\mu)=A_p(\mu,{\mathcal B}_{\mathcal{D}})$ and $RH_p(\mu)=RH_p(\mu,{\mathcal B}_{\mathcal{D}})$.
<!-- end lemma-10 -->
<!-- proof-12: body_md -->
For the second inclusion, let $\Delta\subset\mathring Q_0$ be a restricted
ball and let $w\in A_p(\mu,{\mathcal B}_{Q_0})$. Since $\Delta\subset{\widetilde{Q}}_\Delta$ and
$\mu({\widetilde{Q}}_\Delta)\leq\widehat C_\mu\mu(\Delta)$ by
{(\ref{eqDoublingConstantsModified})}, both averages over $\Delta$ are bounded by
$\widehat C_\mu$ times the corresponding averages over ${\widetilde{Q}}_\Delta$, so
$$\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_{\Delta}w\, d\mu
\left(\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_{\Delta}w^{\frac1{1-p}}\, d\mu\right)^{p-1}
\leq\widehat C_\mu^{\,p}\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_{{\widetilde{Q}}_\Delta}w\, d\mu
\left(\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_{{\widetilde{Q}}_\Delta}w^{\frac1{1-p}}\, d\mu\right)^{p-1}\leq C .$$
The first inclusion is identical, with $Q\subset B_Q\cap X\subset V_{Q_0}$ in
place of $\Delta\subset{\widetilde{Q}}_\Delta$, and the $RH_p$ statements are obtained
in the same way.
<!-- end proof-12 -->
<!-- lemma-11: body_md -->
\label{lemDyadicToBallsAinfty}
Let $\mu$ be a doubling Radon measure in ${\mathbb R}^d$, let $Q_0\in{\mathcal D}\cup3{\mathcal D}$ and
let $0<\delta,{\varepsilon}<1$. Then
$$QC_{1-\widehat C_\mu^{-1}\delta,\,1-{\varepsilon}}(\mu,V_{Q_0})
\subset QC_{1-\delta,\,1-{\varepsilon}}(\mu,{\mathcal B}_{Q_0})
\subset QC_{1-\widehat C_\mu\delta,\,1-{\varepsilon}}(\mu,\mathring Q_0).$$
In particular
$$A_\infty^{QC}(\mu,V_{Q_0})\subset A_\infty^{QC}(\mu,{\mathcal B}_{Q_0})
\subset A_\infty^{QC}(\mu,\mathring Q_0),$$
and therefore $A_\infty^{QC}(\mu)=A_\infty^{QC}(\mu,{\mathcal B}_{\mathcal{D}})$.
<!-- end lemma-11 -->
<!-- proof-13: body_md -->
By {(\ref{eqDefQCbis})}, membership in $QC_{1-\eta,1-{\varepsilon}}$ means that
$\mu(E)>\eta\,\mu(B)$ implies $\nu(E)\geq{\varepsilon}\,\nu(B)$ for every Borel
$E\subset B$ in the relevant basis. Let then
$\nu\in QC_{1-\widehat C_\mu^{-1}\delta,1-{\varepsilon}}(\mu,V_{Q_0})$ and let
$E\subset Q\in{\mathcal B}_{Q_0}$ with $\mu(E)>\delta\,\mu(Q)$. By
{(\ref{eqDoublingConstantsModified})} we have
$\mu(Q)\geq\widehat C_\mu^{-1}\mu(B_Q\cap X)$, so
$\mu(E)>\widehat C_\mu^{-1}\delta\,\mu(B_Q\cap X)$, and since
$B_Q\cap X\subset V_{Q_0}$ the hypothesis yields
$\nu(E)\geq{\varepsilon}\,\nu(B_Q\cap X)\geq{\varepsilon}\,\nu(Q)$. The second inclusion is the
same argument with $\Delta\subset{\widetilde{Q}}_\Delta$ in place of
$Q\subset B_Q\cap X$.
<!-- end proof-13 -->
<!-- lemma-12: body_md -->
\label{lemQCDoubling}
Let $\mu$ be a doubling Radon measure in ${\mathbb R}^d$ and let
$Q_0\in{\mathcal D}\cup3{\mathcal D}$. Then
$$QC^{d}_{\delta,1-{\varepsilon}}(\mu,\mathring Q_0)
\subset QC^{d}_{\widehat C_\mu^{-1}\delta,\,1-\widehat C_\nu^{-1}{\varepsilon}}(\mu,{\mathcal B}_{Q_0}),$$
and conversely with the two bases interchanged, $\widehat C_\nu$ being the constant
of {(\ref{eqDoublingConstantsModified})} for $\nu$. In particular
$QC^{d}(\mu,{\mathcal B}_{\mathcal{D}})=QC^{d}(\mu)$.
<!-- end lemma-12 -->
<!-- proof-14: body_md -->
Let $\nu\in QC^{d}_{\delta,1-{\varepsilon}}(\mu,\mathring Q_0)$ and let
$E\subset Q\in{\mathcal B}_{Q_0}$ be Borel. If $\nu(E\cap\Delta_Q)\leq(1-{\varepsilon})
\nu(\Delta_Q)$ then $\nu(\Delta_Q\setminus E)\geq{\varepsilon}\,\nu(\Delta_Q)$, and the
doubling of $\nu$ gives $\nu(Q\setminus E)\geq\widehat C_\nu^{-1}{\varepsilon}\,\nu(Q)$;
contrapositively,
$$\nu(E)>\left(1-\widehat C_\nu^{-1}{\varepsilon}\right)\nu(Q)
\implies\nu(E\cap\Delta_Q)>(1-{\varepsilon})\,\nu(\Delta_Q).$$
Applying the hypothesis to the ball $\Delta_Q\subset\mathring Q_0$, in the
form {(\ref{eqDefQCbis})}, and then the doubling of $\mu$, we get
$\mu(E\cap\Delta_Q)\geq\delta\,\mu(\Delta_Q)$ and hence
$\mu(E)\geq\widehat C_\mu^{-1}\delta\,\mu(Q)$, which is the assertion. The converse
is the same with $Q_\Delta\subset\Delta$ in place of $\Delta_Q\subset Q$.
<!-- end proof-14 -->
<!-- proposition-1: body_md -->
\label{propoDoublingAllEquivalent}
If $\mu$ is a doubling Radon measure in ${\mathbb R}^d$ and ${\mathcal D}$ is a dyadic grid
associated to $X={\operatorname{supp}}\mu$, then
$$A_\infty^{U}(\mu,{\mathcal B}_{\mathcal{D}})=A_\infty^{QC}(\mu,{\mathcal B}_{\mathcal{D}})
=QC^{d}(\mu,{\mathcal B}_{\mathcal{D}})=QC^{d}(\mu)=A_\infty^{QC}(\mu)=A_\infty^{U}(\mu).$$
<!-- end proposition-1 -->
<!-- proof-15: body_md -->
Lemmas \ref{lemTrivialImplications} and \ref{lemDyadicToBallsAinfty} give
$$A_\infty^{U}(\mu,V_{Q_0})\subset A_\infty^{QC}(\mu,V_{Q_0})
\subset A_\infty^{QC}(\mu,{\mathcal B}_{Q_0}),$$
while {(\ref{eqBothDoubling})} and Lemma \ref{lemApDyadicornot} give
$$A_\infty^{QC}(\mu,{\mathcal B}_{Q_0})\stackrel{\text{\color{grisclar}{(\ref{eqBothDoubling})}}} =
A_\infty^{U}(\mu,{\mathcal B}_{Q_0})\subset A_\infty^{U}(\mu,\mathring Q_0).$$
More precisely, a weight $\nu\in A_\infty^{QC}(\mu,{\mathcal B}_{\mathcal{D}})$ lies in
$A_q(\mu,\mathring Q_0)$ with $q$ depending only on the $A_\infty^{QC}$
constants, which are independent of $Q_0$; since every restricted ball is
contained in some $\mathring Q_0$ with $Q_0\in3{\mathcal D}$ of comparable radius
(Remark \ref{remDimensionalConstant}), this gives $\nu\in A_q(\mu)$. Hence
$$\begin{aligned}
\nu\in A_\infty^{U}(\mu)&\implies\nu\in A_\infty^{QC}(\mu)
\implies\nu\in A_\infty^{QC}(\mu,{\mathcal B}_{\mathcal{D}})\\
&\implies\nu\in A_\infty^{U}(\mu,{\mathcal B}_{\mathcal{D}})\implies\nu\in A_\infty^{U}(\mu),
\end{aligned}$$
which gives the first, second, fifth and sixth equalities. The remaining ones are
{(\ref{eqBothDoubling})} and Lemma \ref{lemQCDoubling}.
<!-- end proof-15 -->
<!-- corollary-1: body_md -->
\label{coroDoublingAllEquivalentDual}
If $\mu$ is a Radon measure in ${\mathbb R}^d$ and ${\mathcal D}$ is a dyadic grid associated to
${\operatorname{supp}}\mu$, then
$$RH_1^{U,d}(\mu,{\mathcal B}_{\mathcal{D}})=RH_1^{QC,d}(\mu,{\mathcal B}_{\mathcal{D}})=RH_1^{QC,d}(\mu)
=RH_1^{U,d}(\mu),$$
and these classes are empty unless $\mu$ is doubling. Consequently, if $\mu$
is doubling,
$$A_\infty^{U}(\mu)=A_\infty^{QC}(\mu)=QC^{d}(\mu)=RH_1^{QC,d}(\mu)
=RH_1^{U,d}(\mu).$$
<!-- end corollary-1 -->
<!-- remark-8: body_md -->
\label{remAllAreTheSame}
Thus, when $\mu$ and $\nu$ are doubling measures with common support, all the
conditions of \S\ref{secClasses} agree, and to verify any of them it suffices
to verify a reverse H\"older inequality, or to bound an $A_p$ constant, or to
exhibit a single pair $(\delta,{\varepsilon})$ with $\nu\in QC_{\delta,{\varepsilon}}(\mu)$, or
the same on the basis ${\mathcal D}\cup3{\mathcal D}$. In this generality the statement is due
to Korte and Kansanen \cite[Cor.~4.19, Rem.~4.20]{KorteKansanen}, for complete
doubling metric measure spaces.
<!-- end remark-8 -->
<!-- remark-9: body_md -->
\label{remQCnotQCD}
Proposition \ref{propoDoublingAllEquivalent} does not yield
$QC(\mu)=QC(\mu,{\mathcal B}_{\mathcal{D}})$. Writing $\delta=1-\delta_0$, the first inclusion
of Lemma \ref{lemDyadicToBallsAinfty} sends
$$QC_{\delta,{\varepsilon}}(\mu)\quad\text{into}\quad
QC_{1-\widehat C_\mu\delta_0,\,{\varepsilon}}(\mu,{\mathcal B}_{Q_0}),$$
and the new parameter is
admissible only when $\delta_0<\widehat C_\mu^{-1}$; a measure that belongs to
$QC_{\delta,{\varepsilon}}(\mu)$ only for small $\delta$ therefore need not belong to
any $QC_{\delta',{\varepsilon}'}(\mu,{\mathcal B}_{Q_0})$. The class $A_\infty^{QC}$ is
unaffected, since there $\delta$ may be taken as close to $1$ as one pleases;
$RH_1^{QC}$ is not, and this is why Corollary
\ref{coroDoublingAllEquivalentDual} has to be obtained by duality rather than
directly. Whether $QC(\mu)=QC(\mu,{\mathcal B}_{\mathcal{D}})$ we do not know; see
\S\ref{secOpen}.
<!-- end remark-9 -->
<!-- proposition-2: body_md -->
\label{propoThinAnnuli}
Let $\mu$ be a doubling Radon measure in ${\mathbb R}^d$ with thin annuli and let
$\nu\in QC_{\delta,{\varepsilon}}(\mu)$ for \emph{some} pair $0<\delta,{\varepsilon}<1$. Then
$\nu$ is doubling, with constant $(1-{\varepsilon})^{-N}$, where
$N=\lceil\log2/\log(1+\eta)\rceil$ and $\eta$ is the parameter associated with
$\theta=\delta/2$ in {(\ref{eqThinAnnuli})}.
<!-- end proposition-2 -->
<!-- proof-16: body_md -->
Put $\Delta'=\Delta_r(x)$ and $\Delta=\Delta_{(1+\eta)r}(x)$. The set
$E=\Delta\setminus\Delta'$ satisfies
$\mu(E)\leq\frac\delta2\mu(\Delta)<\delta\,\mu(\Delta)$, so {(\ref{eqDefQC})} gives
$\nu(E)\leq{\varepsilon}\,\nu(\Delta)$ and hence $\nu(\Delta')\geq(1-{\varepsilon})\nu(\Delta)$.
Iterating $N$ times yields
$\nu(\Delta_r(x))\geq(1-{\varepsilon})^{N}\nu(\Delta_{2r}(x))$.
<!-- end proof-16 -->
<!-- corollary-2: body_md -->
\label{coroThinAnnuli}
If $\mu$ is a doubling Radon measure in ${\mathbb R}^d$ with thin annuli, then
$$A_\infty^{U}(\mu)=A_\infty^{QC}(\mu)=QC(\mu)=RH_1^{QC}(\mu)=RH_1^{U}(\mu),$$
and the superscript $^{d}$ may be omitted throughout. Combined with
\cite[Thm.~5.2]{AndersonWeirich}, such a $\mu$ also satisfies the strong
Gehring lemma.
<!-- end corollary-2 -->
<!-- proof-17: body_md -->
By Proposition \ref{propoThinAnnuli} every $\nu\in QC(\mu)$ is doubling, so
$QC(\mu)=QC^{d}(\mu)$, which Proposition \ref{propoDoublingAllEquivalent}
identifies with $A_\infty^{U}(\mu)$ and $A_\infty^{QC}(\mu)$. In particular
$RH_1^{QC}(\mu)\subset QC(\mu)$ consists of doubling measures, so Corollary
\ref{coroDoublingAllEquivalentDual} gives
$$RH_1^{QC}(\mu)=RH_1^{QC,d}(\mu)=RH_1^{U,d}(\mu)=RH_1^{U}(\mu).\pushQED{\qed}\qedhere\popQED$$\renewcommand{}{}\vspace{-1em}
<!-- end proof-17 -->
<!-- remark-10: body_md -->
\label{remNoDyadicAnalogue}
Proposition \ref{propoThinAnnuli} has no counterpart on the basis
${\mathcal D}\cup3{\mathcal D}$, and the reason is not that dyadic cubes have bad boundaries.
Its proof goes from $\Delta_r(x)$ to $\Delta_{2r}(x)$ in $N$ steps, each of
which costs a factor $1-{\varepsilon}$ because the annulus it adds carries little
$\mu$-mass; this needs a continuum of radii, and over ${\mathcal D}\cup3{\mathcal D}$ there is
nothing between $Q$ and $\widehat Q$, nor between $Q$ and $3Q$.

What the dyadic setting has instead is Lemma \ref{lemBothDoubling}, which
reaches the same conclusion in a single step and pays for it with a threshold:
the set $3Q\cap Q_0\setminus R$ removed in that step is a fraction of
$3Q\cap Q_0$ that is only known to be at most $1-C_\mu^{-1}$, so
{(\ref{eqDefQC})} is of use there only when $\delta_0>1-C_\mu^{-1}$. This is why
the two halves of {(\ref{eqTrivialImplications})} behave differently:
$A_\infty^{QC}$ quantifies over every $\delta$ and therefore meets the
threshold, so it forces $\nu$ to be ${\mathcal D}$-doubling, whereas $RH_1^{QC}$ is
given its $\delta$ and may well miss it. Example \ref{exStaircase} shows that
it does: there $\nu$ satisfies {(\ref{eqDefQC})} for every small $\delta$ and is
not ${\mathcal D}$-doubling. 
                                       

Restoring the small steps would mean replacing $3{\mathcal D}$ by a family of
enlargements $(1+{\varepsilon})^j Q$ assembled from triples of descendants of $Q$ of a deep
generation inside $3Q$. Such a family has worse nesting properties because intermediate cubes are no longer made of unions of cubes of the
generation of $Q$, and both properties are used throughout; see Remark
\ref{remAdjacentSystems}. We have not studied it.
<!-- end remark-10 -->
<!-- example-2: body_md -->
\label{exLadder}
Without thin annuli the conclusion of Corollary \ref{coroThinAnnuli} fails,
already on a closed subset of ${\mathbb R}$ and with restricted Euclidean balls. Put
$$X:=\{0\}\cup\{2^{k}:k\in{\mathbb Z}\},\qquad
\mu:=\sum_{k\in{\mathbb Z}}2^{k}\delta_{2^{k}},$$
$$b_k:=2^{k|k|},\qquad w(2^{k}):=b_k2^{-k},$$
so that $\nu=w\mu=\sum_{k\in{\mathbb Z}}b_k\delta_{2^{k}}$. Then $X$ is closed, $\mu$
is a doubling Radon measure with ${\operatorname{supp}}\mu=X$, and $w>0$ away from the origin, while
$$w\in RH_\infty(\mu)\subset RH_1^{U}(\mu)
\qquad\text{and}\qquad \nu\text{ is not doubling} .$$
In particular $w\notin A_\infty^{U}(\mu)$, so $RH_1^{U}(\mu)$ is strictly
larger than $RH_1^{U,d}(\mu)$ and the superscript cannot be dropped. We leave the computations to the reader.
<!-- end example-2 -->
<!-- remark-11: body_md -->
\label{remLadderNonAtomic}
The atoms are a convenience, not the point. Replacing each $2^{k}$ by a closed
interval $P_k$ centred there, of length $\delta_k$ much smaller than the gap
$2^{k-1}$ and carrying mass $2^{k}$ distributed uniformly, produces a
non-atomic example with the same properties: a ball meeting $P_k$ and reaching
anything else has radius larger than the gap and therefore contains $P_k$
entirely, while a ball meeting only $P_k$ sees a constant weight. Such a $\mu$
is of course not Ahlfors regular, and cannot be: the gaps between consecutive
scales are what makes the annuli fat.
<!-- end remark-11 -->
<!-- definition-8: body_md -->
\label{defWeakClasses}
Let $\mu$ be a Radon measure in ${\mathbb R}^d$, let $X={\operatorname{supp}}\mu$, let $V\subset X$ be
relatively open and let $\nu$ be a Radon measure supported in $X$.
\begin{itemize}
	\item Given $1<p<\infty$, we say that $\nu\in wRH_p(\mu,V)$ if $\nu\ll\mu$,
	the density $w=\frac{d\nu}{d\mu}$ is positive $\mu$-a.e., and
	\begin{equation}\label{eqWeakRH}
		\left(\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_{\Delta}w^p\, d\mu\right)^{\frac1p}
		\leq\frac{C_{RH}}{\mu(\Delta)}\int_{2\Delta}w\, d\mu
		=C_{RH}\,\frac{\nu(2\Delta)}{\mu(\Delta)}
		\qquad\text{whenever }2\Delta\subset V .
	\end{equation}
	\item Given $0<\delta_0,{\varepsilon}_0<1$, we say that
	$\nu\in wQC_{\delta_0,{\varepsilon}_0}(\mu,V)$ if
	\begin{equation}\label{eqWeakQC}
		\mu(E\cap\Delta)<\delta_0\,\mu(\Delta)
		\implies\nu(E\cap\Delta)\leq{\varepsilon}_0\,\nu(2\Delta)
	\end{equation}
	for every Borel set $E$ and every restricted ball $\Delta$ with $2\Delta\subset V$.
	\item We say that $\nu\in wBL_{{\varepsilon}_0}(\mu,V)$ if {(\ref{eqBLintro})} holds for
	some $0<\delta_0<1$ and $C_0\geq1$, as in Definition \ref{defBLintro}.
\end{itemize}
Finally we put
$$\begin{aligned}
wRH_1^{U}(\mu,V)&\coloneqq \bigcup_{1<p<\infty}wRH_p(\mu,V),\\
wRH_1^{BL}(\mu,V)&\coloneqq \bigcap_{0<{\varepsilon}<1}wBL_{{\varepsilon}}(\mu,V),\\
wRH_1^{QC}(\mu,V)&\coloneqq \bigcap_{0<{\varepsilon}<1}\bigcup_{0<\delta<1}
wQC_{\delta,{\varepsilon}}(\mu,V) .
\end{aligned}$$
If one of these conditions holds for every relatively open $V\subset X$ we
omit $V$ from the notation.
<!-- end definition-8 -->
<!-- remark-12: body_md -->
\label{remExpExample}
Let $\mu={\mathcal L}^1$ on ${\mathbb R}$ and $w(x)=e^{x}$. For an interval $I$ of length $\ell$
one has $\left(\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_{I}w^p\right)^{1/p}\big/\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_{I}w\approx
p^{-1/p}\ell^{1/p'}$ as $\ell\to\infty$, so $w\notin RH_p({\mathcal L}^1)$ for any
$p>1$; equivalently, $\nu=w\,{\mathcal L}^1$ is not doubling, so
$w\notin A_\infty^{U}({\mathcal L}^1)$ either. On the other hand the average of $w$
over $2I$ exceeds that over $I$ by a factor $e^{\ell/2}/2$, which absorbs the
loss, and $w\in wRH_p({\mathcal L}^1)$ for every $p<\infty$. Thus
$RH_1^{U}\subsetneq wRH_1^{U}$ already for the Lebesgue measure. This is
\cite[Ex.~2.4]{KinnunenKurkiMudarra}; see also
\cite[Ex.~3.2]{AndersonHytonenTapiola}. The same is true over the basis
${\mathcal D}\cup3{\mathcal D}$, and there the dilation is redundant precisely when $\nu$ is
doubling; see Remark \ref{remWeakDyadicDoubling} and Example
\ref{exWeakNotStrong}.
<!-- end remark-12 -->
<!-- lemma-13: body_md -->
\label{lemWeakTrivial1}
Let $1<p<\infty$ and let $\nu\in wRH_p(\mu,V)$ with constant $C_{RH}$. Then
$\nu\in wQC_{\delta,{\varepsilon}}(\mu,V)$ for every $0<{\varepsilon}<1$, with
$\delta=\left(C_{RH}^{-1}{\varepsilon}\right)^{p'}$. In particular
$$wRH_1^{U}(\mu,V)\subset wRH_1^{QC}(\mu,V).$$
<!-- end lemma-13 -->
<!-- proof-18: body_md -->
Let $E\subset\Delta$ be Borel with $2\Delta\subset V$. By H\"older's
inequality and {(\ref{eqWeakRH})},
$$\nu(E)=\int_E w\, d\mu
\leq\left(\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_{\Delta}w^{p}\, d\mu\right)^{\frac1p}
\mu(\Delta)^{\frac1p}\mu(E)^{1-\frac1p}
\leq C_{RH}\,\nu(2\Delta)
\left(\frac{\mu(E)}{\mu(\Delta)}\right)^{\frac1{p'}} ,$$
so $\mu(E)<\delta\mu(\Delta)$ with $\delta=(C_{RH}^{-1}{\varepsilon})^{p'}$ forces
$\nu(E)\leq{\varepsilon}\,\nu(2\Delta)$.
<!-- end proof-18 -->
<!-- lemma-14: body_md -->
\label{lemWeakTrivial2}
Let $\nu\in wQC_{\delta_0,{\varepsilon}_0}(\mu,V)$. Then $\nu$ satisfies {(\ref{eqBLintro})}
with the same ${\varepsilon}_0$, with $\delta_0$ replaced by
$\delta_0\frac{\mu(\Delta)}{\mu(2\Delta)}$ and with $C_0=1+{\varepsilon}_0$. In
particular, if $\mu$ is doubling then
$$wQC_{\delta,{\varepsilon}}(\mu,V)\subset wBL_{{\varepsilon}}(\mu,V)
\qquad\text{and}\qquad
wRH_1^{QC}(\mu,V)\subset wRH_1^{BL}(\mu,V).$$
<!-- end lemma-14 -->
<!-- proof-19: body_md -->
Let $E\subset2\Delta\subset V$ be Borel with
$\mu(2\Delta\cap E)<\delta_0\frac{\mu(\Delta)}{\mu(2\Delta)}\mu(2\Delta)$.
Then $\mu(\Delta\cap E)\leq\mu(2\Delta\cap E)<\delta_0\mu(\Delta)$, so
{(\ref{eqWeakQC})} gives $\nu(E\cap\Delta)\leq{\varepsilon}_0\nu(2\Delta)$ and hence
$$\nu(\Delta)=\nu(\Delta\cap E)+\nu(\Delta\setminus E)
