\leq{\varepsilon}_0\nu(2\Delta\cap E)+\left(1+{\varepsilon}_0\right)\nu(2\Delta\setminus E).$$
If $\mu$ is doubling then $\mu(\Delta)/\mu(2\Delta)\geq C_\mu^{-1}$, so the
admissible $\delta_0$ may be taken independent of $\Delta$.
<!-- end proof-19 -->
<!-- definition-9: body_md -->
\label{defWeakDyadic}
Let $\mu$ be a Radon measure in ${\mathbb R}^d$, let ${\mathcal D}$ be a dyadic grid associated
to $X={\operatorname{supp}}\mu$, let $V\subset X$ be relatively open and put
$${\mathcal Q}_V\coloneqq \{Q\in{\mathcal D}:\, 3Q\subset V\} .$$
Given a Radon measure $\nu$ supported in $X$, we say that
$\nu\in wBL_{{\varepsilon}_0}(\mu,{\mathcal Q}_V)$ if there are $0<\delta_0<1$ and $C_0\geq1$
such that
\begin{equation}\label{eqWeakBLDyadic}
	\mu(3Q\cap E)<\delta_0\,\mu(3Q)
	\implies
	\nu(Q)\leq{\varepsilon}_0\,\nu(3Q\cap E)+C_0\,\nu(3Q\setminus E)
\end{equation}
for every $Q\in{\mathcal Q}_V$ and every Borel set $E\subset3Q$; and we say that
$\nu\in wRH_p(\mu,{\mathcal Q}_V)$ if $\nu\ll\mu$, $w=\frac{d\nu}{d\mu}>0$ $\mu$-a.e.\
and
$$\left(\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_{Q}w^p\, d\mu\right)^{\frac1p}
\leq C_{RH}\,\frac{\nu(3Q)}{\mu(Q)}\qquad\text{for every }Q\in{\mathcal Q}_V .$$
<!-- end definition-9 -->
<!-- lemma-15: body_md -->
\label{lemWeakBLDyadic}
Let $\mu$ be a doubling Radon measure in ${\mathbb R}^d$, let $V\subset X$ be
relatively open and let $\nu$ be a Radon measure supported in $X$. There is a
constant $C_{\mathcal D}$ depending only on $d$, $C_\mu$ and the parameters of ${\mathcal D}$ such
that
$$wBL_{{\varepsilon}_0}(\mu,V)\subset wBL_{C_{\mathcal D}{\varepsilon}_0}(\mu,{\mathcal Q}_V)
\qquad\text{whenever }{\varepsilon}_0<C_{\mathcal D}^{-1} .$$
In particular $wRH_1^{BL}(\mu,V)\subset wRH_1^{BL}(\mu,{\mathcal Q}_V)$.
<!-- end lemma-15 -->
<!-- proof-20: body_md -->
Let $Q\in{\mathcal Q}_V$ and let $E\subset3Q$ be Borel. Choose a maximal
$\frac12\ell(Q)$-separated set of points of $Q$ and let $\{\Delta_j\}_j$ be
the restricted balls of radius $\ell(Q)$ centred at them. They cover $Q$; and
since ${\operatorname{diam}} Q\leq2r_Q=A_0\ell(Q)$, their number and the overlap of their
dilates are bounded by a dimensional constant,
$$Q\subset\bigcup_j\Delta_j,\qquad \sum_j\chi_{2\Delta_j}\leq C_{\mathcal D} .$$
Each $2\Delta_j$ is a restricted ball of radius $2\ell(Q)=\frac4{A_0}r_Q$
centred at a point of $Q\subset B_Q$, so
$2\Delta_j\subset\left(\frac4{A_0}+1\right)B_Q\cap X\subset3Q\subset V$ by
N\ref{eqrem3QNeighbors2}. Also $3Q\subset3\lambda B_Q$ by
{(\ref{eqTrivialInclusionDQP})}, and $2\Delta_j$ contains a restricted ball of
radius $\ell(Q)$ centred in $Q$, so iterating the doubling condition a
bounded number $n_d$ of times gives $\mu(3Q)\leq C_\mu^{n_d}\mu(2\Delta_j)$.

Let now $0<\delta_0<1$ and $C_0\geq1$ be the constants of {(\ref{eqBLintro})} for
$\nu$, and set ${\widetilde{\delta}}_0\coloneqq C_\mu^{-n_d}\delta_0$. If
$\mu(3Q\cap E)<{\widetilde{\delta}}_0\mu(3Q)$, then for every $j$
$$\mu(2\Delta_j\cap E)\leq\mu(3Q\cap E)<{\widetilde{\delta}}_0\,\mu(3Q)
\leq\delta_0\,\mu(2\Delta_j),$$
so {(\ref{eqBLintro})} applies to $\Delta_j$ and to $E\cap2\Delta_j$, and summing
in $j$,
$$\begin{aligned}
\nu(Q)&\leq\sum_j\nu(\Delta_j)
\leq{\varepsilon}_0\sum_j\nu(2\Delta_j\cap E)+C_0\sum_j\nu(2\Delta_j\setminus E)\\
&\leq C_{\mathcal D}{\varepsilon}_0\,\nu(3Q\cap E)+C_{\mathcal D} C_0\,\nu(3Q\setminus E).\pushQED{\qed}\qedhere\popQED
\end{aligned}$$\renewcommand{}{}\vspace{-1em}
<!-- end proof-20 -->
<!-- remark-13: body_md -->
\label{remWeakDyadicDoubling}
Let $\mu$ be ${\mathcal D}$-doubling in $Q_0$ with constant $C_\mu$, let $\nu=w\mu$ be
${\mathcal D}$-doubling in $Q_0$ with constant $C_\nu$ and suppose that
$$\left(\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_{Q}w^p\, d\mu\right)^{\frac1p}
\leq C_{RH}\,\frac{\nu(3Q\cap Q_0)}{\mu(Q)}\qquad
\text{for every }Q\in{\mathcal D}(Q_0).$$
Then $\nu\in RH_p(\mu,{\mathcal B}_{Q_0})$ with constant
$C_\mu^{1/p'}C_\nu C_{RH}$. Indeed $Q\in{\mathcal N}(Q)$, so
$\nu(3Q\cap Q_0)\leq C_\nu\nu(Q)$ and the hypothesis becomes the strong
inequality on ${\mathcal D}(Q_0)$; and if $B=3Q\cap Q_0\in3{\mathcal D}(Q_0)$, one can also see that the strong reverse H\"older inequality holds by standard arguments.
<!-- end remark-13 -->
<!-- example-3: body_md -->
\label{exWeakNotStrong}
Without the doubling of $\nu$ the two conditions genuinely differ, and again
the Lebesgue measure suffices. Fix $1<p<\infty$ and $0<s<\frac1p$, and for
$n\geq1$ let
$$g_n(x)\coloneqq \begin{cases}
2^{n(1-s)}|x|^{-s}, & -1\leq x<0,\\
2^{n}, & 0\leq x<2^{-n},\\
1, & 2^{-n}\leq x<1 .
\end{cases}$$
Since $sp<1$, both $\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_{[-1,1)}g_n\, d\mu$ and
$\big(\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_{[-1,1)}g_n^p\, d\mu\big)^{1/p}$ are comparable to
$2^{n(1-s)}$, with constants depending only on $p$ and $s$. Write
$S_i\coloneqq [0,2^{-n+i})$ and $R_i\coloneqq [-2^{-n+i},0)$ for
$0\leq i\leq n$; these are adjacent dyadic cubes of the same generation, so
$R_i\subset3S_i$. A direct computation gives
$$1\leq\int_{S_i}g_n\, d\mu\leq2,\qquad
\int_{S_i}g_n^q\, d\mu\geq2^{n(q-1)},\qquad
\int_{R_i}g_n\, d\mu=\frac{2^{\,i(1-s)}}{1-s} ,$$
whence, for every $q>1$,
$$\frac{\big(\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_{S_i}g_n^q\, d\mu\big)^{\frac1q}}
{\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_{S_i}g_n\, d\mu}\geq\frac{2^{\,i(1-1/q)}}2,
\qquad
\frac{\big(\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_{S_i}g_n^p\, d\mu\big)^{\frac1p}}
{\mu(S_i)^{-1}\int_{3S_i}g_n\, d\mu}
\leq(1-s)\,2^{\,\frac1p-\frac ip+si}\leq2 ,$$
the last step holding because $s<\frac1p$. Hence the weak inequality holds on every
$S_i$ with a constant independent of $n$, while the strong one fails on $S_n$
by a factor $2^{\,n(1-1/q)-1}$.

To assemble a single weight, let $\mu$ be the Lebesgue measure on $X=[0,1]$,
let ${\mathcal D}$ be the standard dyadic grid, put $P_k\coloneqq [2^{-k-1},2^{-k})$
and let $\Phi_k:[-1,1)\to P_k$ be the increasing affine bijection, which maps
the dyadic grid onto ${\mathcal D}(P_k)$. Define
$$w\coloneqq \sum_{k\geq1}
\frac{g_k\circ\Phi_k^{-1}}{\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_{[-1,1)}g_k\, d\mu}\,\chi_{P_k},
\qquad\text{so that }\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_{P_k}w\, d\mu=1\text{ for every }k .$$
Every cube of ${\mathcal D}([0,1))$ is contained in some $P_k$, or is one of the
$T_m\coloneqq [0,2^{-m})=\bigcup_{k\geq m}P_k$. On the first kind the estimates
above apply verbatim; on the second, $\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_{T_m}w\, d\mu=1$ and
$$\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_{T_m}w^p\, d\mu=2^{m}\sum_{k\geq m}2^{-k-1}\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_{P_k}w^p\, d\mu
\leq C(p,s),$$
because $\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_{P_k}w^p\, d\mu=\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_{[-1,1)}g_k^p\, d\mu\big/
\big(\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_{[-1,1)}g_k\, d\mu\big)^{p}$ is bounded independently of $k$.
Hence $w\in wRH_p(\mu,{\mathcal Q}_X)$, while for every $q>1$ the strong inequality
fails inside $P_k$ by a factor $2^{\,k(1-1/q)-1}\to\infty$, so
$w\notin RH_q(\mu,{\mathcal B}_{Q_0})$ for any $q>1$, with $Q_0=[0,1)$. By Remark
\ref{remWeakDyadicDoubling} the measure $\nu=w\mu$ is not ${\mathcal D}$-doubling, as
one also sees directly: $\nu(3S_i)\gtrsim2^{\,i(1-s)}\nu(S_i)$ inside each
$P_k$.
<!-- end example-3 -->
<!-- lemma-16: body_md -->
\label{lemBLAC}
Let $\mu$ be a doubling Radon measure in ${\mathbb R}^d$, let $V\subset X$ be
relatively open and let $\nu\in wBL_{{\varepsilon}_0}(\mu,{\mathcal Q}_V)$ with
${\varepsilon}_0\leq{\varepsilon}_1$, where ${\varepsilon}_1>0$ depends only on $d$ and $C_\mu$. Then
$\nu|_V\ll\mu|_V$.
<!-- end lemma-16 -->
<!-- proof-21: body_md -->
Put $\kappa\coloneqq 2\lambda A_0\rho_0$ and let $N$ be such that
$\mu(\kappa B)\leq C_\mu^{N}\mu(B)$ for every ball $B$ centred on $X$; both
are dimensional. Fix $C_\nu>2C_\mu^{N}$ and let
$$G\coloneqq \left\{x\in{\operatorname{supp}}\nu:\
\liminf_{r\to0}\frac{\nu(\kappa B_r(x))}{\nu(B_r(x))}\geq C_\nu\right\} .$$

We first check that $\nu(G)=0$. If $x\in G$ there is $r_x>0$ with
$\nu(\kappa B_r(x))\geq\frac{C_\nu}2\nu(B_r(x))$ for every $0<r<r_x$, whence,
writing $B=B_r(x)$ and iterating while $\kappa^kr\leq r_x$,
$$\frac{\nu(\kappa^kB)}{\mu(\kappa^kB)}
\geq\left(\frac{C_\nu}{2C_\mu^{N}}\right)^{k}\frac{\nu(B)}{\mu(B)} .$$
For each small $r$ take $k=k(r)$ maximal with $\kappa^kr\leq r_x$; then
$\kappa^kr>r_x/\kappa$, so the left-hand side is at most
$\nu(B_{r_x}(x))/\mu(B_{r_x/\kappa}(x))$, a quantity independent of $r$, while
$k(r)\to\infty$ as $r\to0$. Since $C_\nu>2C_\mu^{N}$ we conclude
\begin{equation}\label{eqLimitBallsE}
	\lim_{r\to0}\frac{\nu(B_r(x))}{\mu(B_r(x))}=0
	\qquad\text{for every }x\in G .
\end{equation}
Let ${\varepsilon}>0$ and, restricting to a bounded portion of $G$ if necessary, let $U$
be open with $G\subset U$ and $\mu(U)<\infty$. By {(\ref{eqLimitBallsE})} every
$x\in G$ carries a ball $B_x\subset U$ centred at $x$ with
$\nu(5B_x)<{\varepsilon}\mu(5B_x)$; extracting by the $5r$-covering theorem a disjoint
subfamily $\{B_k\}$ whose dilates $5B_k$ cover $G$,
$$\nu(G)\leq\sum_k\nu(5B_k)\leq{\varepsilon}\sum_k\mu(5B_k)
\leq C_\mu^{3}{\varepsilon}\sum_k\mu(B_k)\leq C_\mu^{3}{\varepsilon}\,\mu(U),$$
and letting ${\varepsilon}\to0$ gives $\nu(G)=0$.

Suppose now that $\nu|_V\not\ll\mu|_V$, so that there is a Borel set
$F\subset V$ with $\nu(F)>0$ and $\mu(F)=0$. Since $\nu(G)=0$, we may pick a
point $x\in F\setminus G$ which is a point of $\nu$-density of $F$, and a
sequence of balls $B_j=B_{r_j}(x)$ with $r_j\to0$ and
\begin{equation}\label{eqDoublingBalls}
	\nu(\kappa B_j)\leq C_\nu\,\nu(B_j) .
\end{equation}
Cover $B_j\cap X$ by the cubes $\{Q_j^k\}_{k\leq N_d}$ of the generation
determined by $r(B_j)\leq\ell(Q_j^k)<\rho_0 r(B_j)$; their number $N_d$ is
dimensional. Since
$r(B_j)>\rho_0^{-1}\ell(Q_j^k)=2(\rho_0A_0)^{-1}r_{Q_j^k}$, one checks from
{(\ref{eqTrivialInclusionDQP})} that
\begin{equation}\label{eqInclusionsImpressive}
	2B_j\cap X\subset3Q_j^k\subset\kappa B_j .
\end{equation}
Let $Q_j$ be the cube $Q_j^k$ of largest $\nu$-measure, so that
$\nu(B_j)\leq N_d\,\nu(Q_j)$. For $j$ large we have $3Q_j\subset V$, that is
$Q_j\in{\mathcal Q}_V$, because $V$ is relatively open. Combining,
$$\frac1{N_dC_\nu}
\stackrel{\text{\color{grisclar}{(\ref{eqDoublingBalls})}}} \leq\frac{N_d^{-1}\nu(B_j)}{\nu(\kappa B_j)}
\stackrel{\text{\color{grisclar}{(\ref{eqInclusionsImpressive})}}} \leq\frac{N_d^{-1}\nu(B_j)}{\nu(3Q_j)}
\leq\frac{\nu(Q_j)}{\nu(3Q_j)} .$$
As $\mu(F\cap3Q_j)=0<\delta_0\mu(3Q_j)$ whatever $\delta_0$ is,
{(\ref{eqWeakBLDyadic})} applies with $E=F\cap 3Q_j$ and yields
$$\frac1{N_dC_\nu}\leq{\varepsilon}_0\frac{\nu(3Q_j\cap F)}{\nu(3Q_j)}
+C_0\frac{\nu(3Q_j\setminus F)}{\nu(3Q_j)}
\stackrel{\text{\color{grisclar}{(\ref{eqInclusionsImpressive})}}} \leq
{\varepsilon}_0+C_0C_\nu\,\frac{\nu(\kappa B_j\setminus F)}{\nu(\kappa B_j)} .$$
The last quotient tends to $0$ because $x$ is a point of $\nu$-density of $F$,
so letting $j\to\infty$ gives $\frac1{N_dC_\nu}\leq{\varepsilon}_0$. Thus the lemma
holds with ${\varepsilon}_1\coloneqq \left(2N_dC_\nu\right)^{-1}$, which depends only on
$d$ and $C_\mu$.
<!-- end proof-21 -->
<!-- lemma-17: body_md -->
\label{lemInfPhi}
Let $\mu$ be a doubling Radon measure in ${\mathbb R}^d$, let $V\subset X$ be a bounded
relatively open set and let $K\subset V$ be compact. Then
$$\inf_{K}\phi\geq C_\mu^{-k_d-2}
\left(\frac{{\operatorname{diam}} K}{\operatorname{dist}(K,X\setminus V)}\right)
^{-\frac{\log C_\mu}{\log\rho_0}}\mu(K),$$
with $k_d$ depending only on $d$ and on the parameters of ${\mathcal D}$.
<!-- end lemma-17 -->
<!-- proof-22: body_md -->
If $V=X$ then $\phi\equiv\mu(X)\geq\mu(K)$ and there is nothing to prove, so
let $F\coloneqq X\setminus V\neq\varnothing$. Let $x\in K$ and let $Q_x\in{\mathcal W}$
contain $x$. By Wh\ref{itemWhRadius} and the triangle inequality,
$$\operatorname{dist}(K,F)\leq\operatorname{dist}(x,F)\leq\operatorname{dist}(Q_x,F)+{\operatorname{diam}} Q_x
\leq\left(\rho_0(\alpha\lambda+1)+2\right)r_{Q_x},$$
so $r_{Q_x}$ is bounded below by a fixed multiple of $\operatorname{dist}(K,F)$. On the
other hand, writing $S=\mathcal P^{k}(Q_x)$ for the $k$-th ancestor of $Q_x$,
whose exterior radius is $\rho_0^{k}r_{Q_x}$, we have $x\in S\subset B_S$ and
$3S\supset\lambda B_S\cap X$ by {(\ref{eqTrivialInclusionDQP})}, so $3S\supset K$
as soon as $\rho_0^{k}r_{Q_x}\geq\frac{{\operatorname{diam}} K}{\lambda-1}$. Combining the
two, a single $k_0$ with
\begin{equation}\label{eqKZero}
	k_d+\log_{\rho_0}\frac{{\operatorname{diam}} K}{\operatorname{dist}(K,F)}\leq k_0
	\leq k_d+1+\log_{\rho_0}\frac{{\operatorname{diam}} K}{\operatorname{dist}(K,F)}
\end{equation}
serves every $x\in K$. Since $\mu$ is doubling, Lemma
\ref{lemDoublingNeighbors} applies with $Q_0$ arbitrarily large, so its items
hold for every cube; iterating DN\ref{itemDN2} along the chain
$Q_x\subset\widehat{Q_x}\subset\cdots\subset\mathcal P^{k_0}(Q_x)$, and then
DN\ref{itemDN1} once with $T=S$ to pass from $\mathcal P^{k_0}(Q_x)$ to its
triple,
$$\phi(x)=\mu(Q_x)\geq C_\mu^{-k_0}\mu\left(\mathcal P^{k_0}(Q_x)\right)
\geq C_\mu^{-k_0-1}\mu\left(3\mathcal P^{k_0}(Q_x)\right)
\geq C_\mu^{-k_0-1}\mu(K) ,$$
and {(\ref{eqKZero})} turns $C_\mu^{-k_0-1}$ into the stated quantity.
<!-- end proof-22 -->
<!-- proposition-3: body_md -->
\label{propoBL}
Let $\mu$ be a doubling Radon measure in ${\mathbb R}^d$, let $V\subset X$ be a bounded
relatively open set, let $K\subset V$ be compact with $\mu(K)>0$, and let
$\nu\in wBL_{{\varepsilon}_0}(\mu,{\mathcal Q}_V)$ with constants $\delta_0$, $C_0$ and
\begin{equation}\label{eqEpsThreshold}
	{\varepsilon}_0\leq\min\left\{{\varepsilon}_1,\ \left(2+2C_\mu^4\right)^{-1}\right\},
\end{equation}
with ${\varepsilon}_1$ as in Lemma \ref{lemBLAC}, and write $w=\frac{d\nu}{d\mu}$, which
exists by that lemma. Then there is $q_0>1$, depending on $d$, on $C_\mu$ and
on $\delta_0$, $C_0$, such that for every $1<q\leq q_0$
$$\left(\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_{K}w^q\, d\mu\right)^{\frac1q}
\leq C\left(\frac{{\operatorname{diam}} K}{\operatorname{dist}(K,X\setminus V)}\right)
^{\frac{\log C_\mu}{\log\rho_0}}
\left(\frac{\mu(V)}{\mu(K)}\right)^{1+\frac1q}\frac{\nu(V)}{\mu(V)} ,$$
with $C$ depending on $d$, $C_\mu$, $\delta_0$, $C_0$ and $q$.
<!-- end proposition-3 -->
<!-- proof-23: body_md -->
Let ${\mathcal W}$ and $\phi$ be as in {(\ref{eqDefPhi})}. Since $\phi\equiv\mu(Q)$ on each
$Q\in{\mathcal W}$, we have
$\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_Q\phi w\, d\mu=\nu(Q)\leq\nu(V)$, so we may run the
Calder\'on--Zygmund iteration of Lemma \ref{lemCZIteration} inside each
$Q\in{\mathcal W}$ with
$$f=\phi w,\qquad \alpha_0\coloneqq \nu(V),\qquad
\rho\coloneqq C_\mu\tau^{-1},\qquad \alpha_j\coloneqq \rho^{j}\alpha_0,$$
and $0<\tau<1$ to be fixed. Writing $\mathcal F^j$ for the union over
$Q\in{\mathcal W}$ of the resulting stopping families, $U^j\coloneqq
\bigcup_{R\in\mathcal F^j}R$, and
$$A^j\coloneqq \{x\in V:\, \phi(x)w(x)>\alpha_j\},$$
properties CZ\ref{itemCZU}--CZ\ref{itemCZLayers} give, for every $j\geq0$,
\begin{equation}\label{eqCZforBL}
\begin{aligned}
	&\alpha_j<\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_{R}\phi w\, d\mu=\frac{\mu(Q)\nu(R)}{\mu(R)}
	\leq C_\mu\alpha_j
	\qquad\text{for } R\in\mathcal F^j\text{ with } R\subset Q\in{\mathcal W},\\
	&A^j\subset U^j\cup Z^j \qquad\text{with }\mu(Z^j)=0,
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}
together with CZ\ref{itemCZIhat} and $\mu(U^j)\to0$.

\emph{Step 1.} We claim that if
\begin{equation}\label{eqTauSmall}
	\tau<\frac1{2C_0C_\mu^{2}} ,
\end{equation}
then for every $j\geq1$ there is a subfamily $\mathcal G\subset\mathcal F^j$
with
\begin{equation}\label{eqBLFirstStep}
	\begin{cases}
		\mu(A^{j-1}\cap 3P)\geq\delta_0\,\mu(3P) &\text{for every }
		P\in\mathcal G,\\
		U^{j}\subset\bigcup_{P\in\mathcal G}10\lambda^2A_0B_P, &\\
		\sum_{P\in\mathcal G}\chi_{3P}\leq C_{\mathrm B} . &
	\end{cases}
\end{equation}
Consider first the subfamily of cubes whose triples are maximal,
$${\widetilde{\mathcal F}}^j\coloneqq \left\{P\in\mathcal F^j:\
3R\not\supset3P\ \text{ for every }R\in\mathcal F^j\setminus\{P\}\right\} ,$$
which is well defined and non-empty whenever $\mathcal F^j$ is, because $V$ is
bounded; an induction on the generation of the cubes gives
\begin{equation}\label{eqBigcup3PUj}
	\bigcup_{P\in{\widetilde{\mathcal F}}^j}3P=\bigcup_{R\in\mathcal F^j}3R\supset U^j .
\end{equation}
We show that no $P\in{\widetilde{\mathcal F}}^j$ can satisfy
\begin{equation}\label{eqBLFirstStepCont}
	\mu(A^{j-1}\cap3P)<\delta_0\,\mu(3P) .
\end{equation}
Let $P\in{\widetilde{\mathcal F}}^j$, say $P\subset Q\in{\mathcal W}$; note that
$\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_Q\phi w\, d\mu\leq\alpha_0\leq\alpha_j$ forces $P\neq Q$, so
$P\subsetneq Q$. Every $R\in{\mathcal N}(P)\setminus\mathcal F^j$ satisfies
\begin{equation}\label{eqNeighborBelowLevel}
	\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_{R}\phi w\, d\mu\leq\alpha_j .
\end{equation}
Indeed, otherwise $R$ would be contained in a maximal cube
$R'\in\mathcal F^j$ with the same property; as $R\notin\mathcal F^j$ the
inclusion is strict, so $r_{R'}\geq\rho_0 r_R=\rho_0 r_P$, and
N\ref{eqrem3QNeighbors5} would give $3P\subset3R'$ with
$R'\in\mathcal F^j\setminus\{P\}$, contrary to the definition of
${\widetilde{\mathcal F}}^j$.

Let $R\in{\mathcal N}(P)$ and let ${\widetilde{Q}}\in{\mathcal W}$ be the Whitney cube containing it. By
{(\ref{eqNeighborBelowLevel})} and {(\ref{eqCZforBL})}, in either case
$\frac{\mu({\widetilde{Q}})\nu(R)}{\mu(R)}\leq C_\mu\alpha_j$, while {(\ref{eqCZforBL})} at
$P$ gives $\alpha_j<\frac{\mu(Q)\nu(P)}{\mu(P)}$; and Lemma
\ref{lemNeighborWhitney} applied to $P\subsetneq Q$ yields
\begin{equation}\label{eqQwtQSimilar}
	\mu({\widetilde{Q}})\geq C_\mu^{-2}\mu(Q) .
\end{equation}
Combining the three and summing over $R\in{\mathcal N}(P)$, we find that $P$ is
$\nu$-doubling:
\begin{equation}\label{eqPisNuDoubling}
\begin{aligned}
	\nu(3P)&\leq\nu(P)+C_\mu^{3}\frac{\nu(P)}{\mu(P)}
	\sum_{R\in{\mathcal N}(P)}\mu(R)
	=\nu(P)\left(1+C_\mu^{3}\frac{\mu(3P)}{\mu(P)}\right)\\
	&\leq\left(1+C_\mu^{4}\right)\nu(P).
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}
On the other hand, {(\ref{eqBLFirstStepCont})} and {(\ref{eqWeakBLDyadic})} with
$E=A^{j-1}\cap3P$ give
$\nu(P)\leq{\varepsilon}_0\nu(3P\cap A^{j-1})+C_0\nu(3P\setminus A^{j-1})$, so
{(\ref{eqPisNuDoubling})} and ${\varepsilon}_0(1+C_\mu^4)\leq\frac12$ yield
\begin{equation}\label{eqPby3PminusA}
	\nu(P)\leq2C_0\,\nu(3P\setminus A^{j-1}) .
\end{equation}
Finally, {(\ref{eqQwtQSimilar})} gives $\mu(Q)\leq C_\mu^{2}\phi(x)$ for
$x\in3P$, so
$$\frac{\alpha_j\mu(P)}{\mu(Q)}\stackrel{\text{\color{grisclar}{(\ref{eqCZforBL})}}} <\nu(P)
\stackrel{\text{\color{grisclar}{(\ref{eqPby3PminusA})},\ {(\ref{eqQwtQSimilar})}}} \leq
\frac{2C_0C_\mu^{2}}{\mu(Q)}\int_{3P\setminus A^{j-1}}\phi w\, d\mu
\leq\frac{2C_0C_\mu^{3}}{\rho}\,\frac{\alpha_j\mu(P)}{\mu(Q)} ,$$
whence $\tau=C_\mu\rho^{-1}\geq(2C_0C_\mu^{2})^{-1}$, contradicting
{(\ref{eqTauSmall})}. Thus every $P\in{\widetilde{\mathcal F}}^j$ satisfies the first line of
{(\ref{eqBLFirstStep})}, and so does every subfamily of ${\widetilde{\mathcal F}}^j$. Now
apply Lemma \ref{lemBesicovitchSelection} to ${\widetilde{\mathcal F}}^j$, whose cubes
are pairwise disjoint and contained in the bounded set $V$: the resulting
$\mathcal G$ satisfies the third line of {(\ref{eqBLFirstStep})}, and the second
follows from {(\ref{eqBigcup3PUj})}.

\emph{Step 2.} We claim that, with
$C_1\coloneqq 1+C_{\mathrm B}C_\mu{\widetilde{C}}_\mu\delta_0^{-1}$,
\begin{equation}\label{eqBLSecondStep}
	\int_{A^{j-1}}\phi w\, d\mu\leq C_1\,\alpha_j\,\mu(A^{j-1})
	\qquad\text{for every }j\geq1 ,
\end{equation}
with ${\widetilde{C}}_\mu$ as in {(\ref{eqEnlargedBallDoubling})}. Indeed, since
$\mathcal F^j$ is a disjoint family, {(\ref{eqCZforBL})} gives
$$\int_{A^{j}}\phi w\, d\mu\leq\int_{U^{j}}\phi w\, d\mu
=\sum_{P\in\mathcal F^j}\int_{P}\phi w\, d\mu
\leq C_\mu\alpha_j\sum_{P\in\mathcal F^j}\mu(P)=C_\mu\alpha_j\,\mu(U^j),$$
and the three lines of {(\ref{eqBLFirstStep})}, in this order, give
$$\begin{aligned}
\mu(U^j)&\leq\sum_{P\in\mathcal G}\mu\left(10\lambda^2A_0B_P\right)
\leq{\widetilde{C}}_\mu\sum_{P\in\mathcal G}\mu(3P)\\
&\leq\frac{{\widetilde{C}}_\mu}{\delta_0}\sum_{P\in\mathcal G}\mu(3P\cap A^{j-1})
\leq\frac{C_{\mathrm B}{\widetilde{C}}_\mu}{\delta_0}\,\mu(A^{j-1}) .
\end{aligned}$$
Adding
$\int_{A^{j-1}\setminus A^{j}}\phi w\, d\mu\leq\alpha_j\mu(A^{j-1})$ gives
{(\ref{eqBLSecondStep})}.

\emph{Step 3.} Fix now
\begin{equation}\label{eqTauChoice}
	\tau\coloneqq \min\left\{\frac{C_\mu}{4C_1},\ \frac1{4C_0C_\mu^{2}}\right\},
\end{equation}
so that {(\ref{eqTauSmall})} holds and $\rho=C_\mu\tau^{-1}\geq4C_1>2C_1$. Then
{(\ref{eqBLSecondStep})} is exactly hypothesis {(\ref{eqBasicLevels})} of Lemma
\ref{lemLevelToQ} for the measure $\mu|_V$, the function $f=\phi w$ and
$p=1$, so for every $1<q\leq q_0$, with $q_0$ as in {(\ref{eqQZero})},
\begin{equation}\label{eqBLThirdStep}
	\int_{V}(\phi w)^q\, d\mu
	\leq C_{C_1,\rho,1,q}\,\nu(V)^{q-1}\int_{V}\phi\, d\nu
	\leq C_{C_1,\rho,1,q}\,\nu(V)^{q}\,\mu(V),
\end{equation}
where we used $\phi\leq\mu(V)$. Restricting to $K$ and using $w\leq\phi
w/\inf_K\phi$ there,
$$\left(\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_{K}w^q\, d\mu\right)^{\frac1q}
\leq\frac1{\mu(K)^{\frac1q}\inf_{K}\phi}
\left(\int_{V}(\phi w)^q\, d\mu\right)^{\frac1q}
\stackrel{\text{\color{grisclar}{(\ref{eqBLThirdStep})}}} \leq
\frac{C_{C_1,\rho,1,q}^{\frac1q}\,\mu(V)^{\frac1q}\,\nu(V)}
{\mu(K)^{\frac1q}\,\inf_{K}\phi} .$$

Inserting the lower bound for $\inf_K\phi$ given by Lemma \ref{lemInfPhi}
proves the proposition.
<!-- end proof-23 -->
<!-- lemma-18: body_md -->
\label{lemLevelToQ}
Let $\mu$ be a measure on a measurable space, let $V$ be a measurable set, let
$p\geq1$ and let $f\colon V\to[0,\infty]$ be measurable with
$f\in L^p(\mu|_V)$. Let $\alpha_0>0$ and $\rho>1$, put
$\alpha_j\coloneqq \rho^j\alpha_0$ and
$$A^j\coloneqq \{x\in V:\, f(x)>\alpha_j\},\qquad j\geq0 .$$
Assume that, with $\sigma$ as in {(\ref{eqDefSigma})},
\begin{equation}\label{eqBasicLevels}
	\int_{A^{j}}f^p\, d\mu\leq C_1\,\alpha_{j+1}^{\,p-\sigma}
	\int_{A^{j}}f^{\,\sigma}\, d\mu\qquad\text{for every }j\geq0,
\end{equation}
and that
\begin{equation}\label{eqRhoLarge}
	\rho^{\,p-\sigma}>2C_1>2 .
\end{equation}
Then, for every $q>p$ for which
\begin{equation}\label{eqConstantLevelToQ}
	C_{C_1,\rho,p,q}\coloneqq
	\frac{\rho^{q-p}\left(\rho^{\,p-\sigma}-1\right)}
	{\rho^{\,q-\sigma}-1-2C_1\rho^{\,q-\sigma}
		\left(\rho^{\,q-\sigma}-\rho^{\,p-\sigma}\right)}
\end{equation}
is positive,
\begin{equation}\label{eqConclusionLevelToQ}
	\int_{V}f^q\, d\mu\leq C_{C_1,\rho,p,q}\,
	\alpha_0^{q-p}\int_{V}f^p\, d\mu .
\end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-18 -->
<!-- remark-14: body_md -->
\label{remRangeOfQ}
Under {(\ref{eqRhoLarge})}, the quantity {(\ref{eqConstantLevelToQ})} is positive for
every $p<q\leq q_0$, where
\begin{equation}\label{eqQZero}
	q_0\coloneqq p+\frac1{\log\rho}
	\log\left(1+\frac{\rho^{\,p-\sigma}-1}{4C_1\rho^{\,2(p-\sigma)}}\right) .
\end{equation}
Indeed, write $\eta$ for the fraction inside the logarithm. By
{(\ref{eqRhoLarge})} we have $\eta<\left(4C_1\rho^{\,p-\sigma}\right)^{-1}<1$, so
$q\leq q_0$ gives $\rho^{q-p}\leq1+\eta<2$ and hence
$\rho^{\,q-\sigma}=\rho^{q-p}\rho^{\,p-\sigma}<2\rho^{\,p-\sigma}$, whence
$$\begin{aligned}
2C_1\rho^{\,q-\sigma}\left(\rho^{\,q-\sigma}-\rho^{\,p-\sigma}\right)
&=2C_1\rho^{\,q-\sigma}\rho^{\,p-\sigma}\left(\rho^{q-p}-1\right)\\
&\leq4C_1\rho^{\,2(p-\sigma)}\eta=\rho^{\,p-\sigma}-1
<\rho^{\,q-\sigma}-1 .
\end{aligned}$$
Moreover, whenever {(\ref{eqConstantLevelToQ})} is positive it is at least $1$:
writing $u\coloneqq \rho^{\,q-\sigma}$ and $a\coloneqq \rho^{\,p-\sigma}$, so
that $\rho^{q-p}=u/a$, the numerator minus the denominator of
{(\ref{eqConstantLevelToQ})} equals
\begin{equation}\label{eqCAtLeastOne}
	\frac ua\left(a-1\right)-\left(u-1-2C_1u(u-a)\right)
	=\left(u-a\right)\left(2C_1u-\frac1a\right)\geq0 ,
\end{equation}
since $u\geq a>1$ and $2C_1>2$.
<!-- end remark-14 -->
<!-- proof-24: body_md -->
For $t\geq0$ and $N\geq1$ write
$$I_N(t)\coloneqq \int_{A^0\setminus A^N}f^t\, d\mu ,$$
so that $I_N(0)=\mu(A^0\setminus A^N)$. All these quantities are finite: on
$A^0\setminus A^N$ we have $\alpha_0<f\leq\alpha_N$, so
$f^t\leq\max\{\alpha_0^{\,t-p},\alpha_N^{\,t-p}\}f^p$ there and
$I_N(t)\leq\max\{\alpha_0^{\,t-p},\alpha_N^{\,t-p}\}\|f\|_{L^p(\mu|_V)}^p$ for
every $t\geq0$. Arguing in the same way on $A^N$, where $f>\alpha_N$, and on
$A^{j}$, where $f>\alpha_j$ and $\sigma\leq p$,
\begin{equation}\label{eqMuANtoZero}
	\mu(A^N)\leq\alpha_N^{-p}\|f\|_{L^p(\mu|_V)}^p\xrightarrow{N\to\infty}0,
	\qquad
	\int_{A^{j}}f^{\,\sigma}\, d\mu\leq\alpha_j^{\,\sigma-p}
	\|f\|_{L^p(\mu|_V)}^p<\infty .
\end{equation}

\emph{Step 1: a truncated form of the hypothesis.} We claim that
\begin{equation}\label{eqEverythingBegins}
	\int_{A^{j}\setminus A^N}f^p\, d\mu\leq2C_1\,\alpha_{j+1}^{\,p-\sigma}
	\int_{A^{j}\setminus A^N}f^{\,\sigma}\, d\mu
	\qquad\text{for every }0\leq j\leq N-1 .
\end{equation}
If $N=j+1$ this is immediate, and with $1$ in place of $2C_1$: on
$A^{j}\setminus A^{j+1}$ we have $f\leq\alpha_{j+1}$, so
$f^p\leq\alpha_{j+1}^{\,p-\sigma}f^{\,\sigma}$ there. Assume then
$N\geq j+2$. Since $f>\alpha_N$ on $A^N$ and $A^N\subset A^{j}$,
$$\int_{A^N}f^{\,\sigma}\, d\mu\leq\frac1{\alpha_N^{\,p-\sigma}}
\int_{A^N}f^{p}\, d\mu
\leq\frac1{\rho^{(p-\sigma)(N-j-1)}\alpha_{j+1}^{\,p-\sigma}}
\int_{A^{j}}f^{p}\, d\mu ,$$
because $\alpha_N=\rho^{N-j-1}\alpha_{j+1}$. Subtracting this from
{(\ref{eqBasicLevels})}, which is legitimate because
$\int_{A^j}f^\sigma\, d\mu<\infty$, we get
$$\int_{A^{j}\setminus A^N}f^{\,\sigma}\, d\mu
\stackrel{\text{\color{grisclar}{(\ref{eqBasicLevels})}}} \geq\left(\frac1{C_1}
-\frac1{\rho^{(p-\sigma)(N-j-1)}}\right)
\frac1{\alpha_{j+1}^{\,p-\sigma}}\int_{A^{j}}f^{p}\, d\mu .$$
By {(\ref{eqRhoLarge})} and $N-j-1\geq1$ we have
$\rho^{(p-\sigma)(N-j-1)}\geq\rho^{\,p-\sigma}>2C_1$, so the bracket is at
least $(2C_1)^{-1}$, and {(\ref{eqEverythingBegins})} follows because
$A^{j}\setminus A^N\subset A^{j}$.

\emph{Step 2: summation by parts.} For every $t\geq0$ and every measurable
$g\geq0$ with $\int_{A^0\setminus A^N}g\, d\mu<\infty$,
\begin{equation}\label{eqSummationByParts}
	\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\alpha_{j+1}^{t}\int_{A^{j}\setminus A^{j+1}}g\, d\mu
	=\alpha_1^{t}\int_{A^0\setminus A^N}g\, d\mu
	+\left(\rho^{t}-1\right)\sum_{k=1}^{N-1}\alpha_{k}^{t}
	\int_{A^{k}\setminus A^N}g\, d\mu .
\end{equation}
Indeed, writing $a_j\coloneqq \int_{A^{j}\setminus A^{j+1}}g\, d\mu$ and
exchanging the order of summation, the right-hand side equals
$\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}a_j\bigl(\alpha_1^t+(\rho^t-1)\sum_{k=1}^{j}\alpha_k^t\bigr)$,
and the geometric sum telescopes to
$\alpha_1^t+\alpha_0^t\rho^{t}\left(\rho^{jt}-1\right)=\alpha_{j+1}^{t}$.

\emph{Step 3: the bootstrap.} Let $q>p$. Since $f\leq\alpha_{j+1}$ on
$A^{j}\setminus A^{j+1}$,
$$\begin{aligned}
I_N(q)&\leq\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\alpha_{j+1}^{\,q-p}
\int_{A^{j}\setminus A^{j+1}}f^{p}\, d\mu\\
&\stackrel{\text{\color{grisclar}{(\ref{eqSummationByParts})}}} =\alpha_1^{\,q-p}I_N(p)
+\left(\rho^{\,q-p}-1\right)\sum_{k=1}^{N-1}\alpha_{k}^{\,q-p}
\int_{A^{k}\setminus A^N}f^{p}\, d\mu .
\end{aligned}$$
By {(\ref{eqEverythingBegins})} and $\alpha_{k+1}=\rho\alpha_k$, each term of the
last sum obeys
$$\alpha_{k}^{\,q-p}\int_{A^{k}\setminus A^N}f^{p}\, d\mu
\leq2C_1\rho^{\,p-\sigma}\,\alpha_k^{\,q-\sigma}
\int_{A^{k}\setminus A^N}f^{\,\sigma}\, d\mu .$$
Writing
$$\Theta\coloneqq \frac{2C_1\rho^{\,p-\sigma}\left(\rho^{\,q-p}-1\right)}
{\rho^{\,q-\sigma}-1}
=\frac{2C_1\left(\rho^{\,q-\sigma}-\rho^{\,p-\sigma}\right)}
{\rho^{\,q-\sigma}-1},$$
and applying {(\ref{eqSummationByParts})} again, now with $t=q-\sigma$ and
$g=f^{\,\sigma}$, we obtain
$$I_N(q)\leq\alpha_1^{\,q-p}I_N(p)+\Theta\left(
\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\alpha_{j+1}^{\,q-\sigma}
\int_{A^{j}\setminus A^{j+1}}f^{\,\sigma}\, d\mu
-\alpha_1^{\,q-\sigma}I_N(\sigma)\right).$$
Two bounds now close the argument. On $A^{j}$ we have
$\alpha_{j+1}=\rho\alpha_{j}<\rho f$, so
$\alpha_{j+1}^{\,q-\sigma}f^{\,\sigma}\leq\rho^{\,q-\sigma}f^{q}$ and the sum
inside the bracket is at most $\rho^{\,q-\sigma}I_N(q)$; and
{(\ref{eqEverythingBegins})} with $j=0$ reads
$I_N(p)\leq2C_1\alpha_1^{\,p-\sigma}I_N(\sigma)$, that is,
$\alpha_1^{\,q-\sigma}I_N(\sigma)\geq(2C_1)^{-1}\alpha_1^{\,q-p}I_N(p)$.
Therefore
$$I_N(q)\left(1-\Theta\rho^{\,q-\sigma}\right)
\leq\alpha_1^{\,q-p}I_N(p)\left(1-\frac{\Theta}{2C_1}\right),$$
and since
$$1-\frac{\Theta}{2C_1}=\frac{\rho^{\,p-\sigma}-1}{\rho^{\,q-\sigma}-1},$$
the factor on the right is always positive, while the one on the left is
positive precisely when the denominator of {(\ref{eqConstantLevelToQ})} is. In that
case, using $\alpha_1^{\,q-p}=\rho^{\,q-p}\alpha_0^{\,q-p}$,
\begin{equation}\label{eqINqbyINp}
	I_N(q)\leq C_{C_1,\rho,p,q}\,\alpha_0^{\,q-p}I_N(p) .
\end{equation}

\emph{Step 4: conclusion.} Since $f\leq\alpha_0$ on $V\setminus A^0$,
$$\int_{V}f^q\, d\mu\leq\alpha_0^{\,q-p}\int_{V\setminus A^0}f^{p}\, d\mu
+\int_{A^0}f^{q}\, d\mu .$$
The sets $A^0\setminus A^N$ increase to $A^0\setminus\bigcap_{N}A^N$, and
$\mu\bigl(\bigcap_{N}A^N\bigr)=0$ by {(\ref{eqMuANtoZero})}, so
$I_N(q)\to\int_{A^0}f^q\, d\mu$ and $I_N(p)\to\int_{A^0}f^p\, d\mu$ by
monotone convergence. Letting $N\to\infty$ in {(\ref{eqINqbyINp})} we get
$$\int_{V}f^q\, d\mu\leq\alpha_0^{\,q-p}\left(
\int_{V\setminus A^0}f^{p}\, d\mu
+C_{C_1,\rho,p,q}\int_{A^0}f^{p}\, d\mu\right),$$
and {(\ref{eqConclusionLevelToQ})} follows because $C_{C_1,\rho,p,q}\geq1$ by
Remark \ref{remRangeOfQ}.
<!-- end proof-24 -->
<!-- lemma-19: body_md -->
\label{lemGehringEnlarged}
Let $\mu$ be a doubling Radon measure in ${\mathbb R}^d$, let $V\subset X$ be a bounded
relatively open set, let $K\subset V$ be compact with
$\mu(K)>0$, let $1<p<\infty$ and let $\nu\in wRH_p(\mu,{\mathcal Q}_V)$ with constant
$C_{RH}$, in the sense of Definition \ref{defWeakDyadic}. Then there is
$q_0>p$, depending on $d$, $C_\mu$, $p$ and $C_{RH}$, such that for every
$p<q\leq q_0$
$$\left(\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_{K}w^{q}\, d\mu\right)^{\frac1q}
\leq C\left(\frac{{\operatorname{diam}} K}{\operatorname{dist}(K,X\setminus V)}\right)
^{\frac{\log C_\mu}{\log\rho_0}}
\left(\frac{\mu(V)}{\mu(K)}\right)^{1+\frac1q}
\left(\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_{V}w^{p}\, d\mu\right)^{\frac1p} ,$$
with $C$ depending on $d$, $C_\mu$, $p$, $C_{RH}$ and $q$.
<!-- end lemma-19 -->
<!-- proof-25: body_md -->
Let ${\mathcal W}$ be a Whitney decomposition of $V$, or ${\mathcal W}\coloneqq \{X\}$ if $V=X$,
and let $\phi$ be as in {(\ref{eqDefPhi})}. Put
$$\alpha_0\coloneqq \mu(V)\left(\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_{V}w^{p}\, d\mu\right)^{\frac1p},
\qquad \alpha_j\coloneqq \rho^{j}\alpha_0,\qquad
\rho\coloneqq C_\mu^{2}\tau^{-1},$$
with $0<\tau<1$ to be fixed. Since $\phi\equiv\mu(Q)$ on $Q\in{\mathcal W}$,
$$\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_{Q}(\phi w)^p\, d\mu=\mu(Q)^{p-1}\int_{Q}w^p\, d\mu
\leq\mu(V)^{p-1}\int_{V}w^p\, d\mu=\alpha_0^{p},$$
so we may run the Calder\'on--Zygmund iteration of Lemma \ref{lemCZIteration}
inside each $Q\in{\mathcal W}$ with $f=(\phi w)^p$ and levels $\alpha_j^{p}$; in the
notation of that lemma this amounts to replacing $\tau$ by
$C_\mu^{1-2p}\tau^{p}$, which is admissible because $\rho^{p}\geq C_\mu$. We
write $\mathcal F^j$, $U^j$ as in \S\ref{secBL} and
$$A^j\coloneqq \{x\in V:\, \phi(x)w(x)>\alpha_j\}\subset U^j
\qquad (j\geq-1,\ U^{-1}\coloneqq V).$$

\emph{Step 1.} We claim that if
\begin{equation}\label{eqTauSmallGehring}
	\tau\leq\frac1{2C_{RH}C_\mu},
\end{equation}
then every $P\in\mathcal F^j$ satisfies
\begin{equation}\label{eqGehringStep1}
	\alpha_j\,\mu(3P)<2C_{RH}C_\mu^{3}\int_{3P\cap A^{j-1}}\phi w\, d\mu .
\end{equation}
Let $P\in\mathcal F^j$ and let $Q\in{\mathcal W}$ contain it; as in \S\ref{secBL},
$\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_Q(\phi w)^p\, d\mu\leq\alpha_0^p\leq\alpha_j^p$ and CZ\ref{itemCZU}
force $P\subsetneq Q$, so Lemma \ref{lemNeighborWhitney} gives
$\mu(Q)\leq C_\mu^{2}\phi(x)$ for every $x\in3P$. Hence, by
CZ\ref{itemCZU}, by Definition \ref{defWeakDyadic} and by
$\mu(3P)\leq C_\mu\mu(P)$,
$$\begin{aligned}
\alpha_j&<\left(\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_{P}(\phi w)^p\, d\mu\right)^{\frac1p}
=\mu(Q)\left(\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_{P}w^p\, d\mu\right)^{\frac1p}\\
&\leq C_{RH}\,\mu(Q)\,\frac{\nu(3P)}{\mu(P)}
\leq C_{RH}C_\mu^{3}\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_{3P}\phi w\, d\mu .
\end{aligned}$$
Splitting the last average over $A^{j-1}$ and its complement, where
$\phi w\leq\alpha_{j-1}=C_\mu^{-2}\tau\alpha_j$, gives
$$\alpha_j\,\mu(3P)<C_{RH}C_\mu^{3}\int_{3P\cap A^{j-1}}\phi w\, d\mu
+C_{RH}C_\mu\tau\,\alpha_j\,\mu(3P),$$
and {(\ref{eqTauSmallGehring})} lets the last term be absorbed, proving
{(\ref{eqGehringStep1})}.

\emph{Step 2.} We claim that, with
$C_1\coloneqq 1+2C_{\mathrm B}C_{RH}C_\mu^{4}{\widetilde{C}}_\mu$ and ${\widetilde{C}}_\mu$ as in
{(\ref{eqEnlargedBallDoubling})},
\begin{equation}\label{eqGehringSecondStep}
	\int_{A^{j-1}}(\phi w)^p\, d\mu
	\leq C_1\,\alpha_j^{\,p-1}\int_{A^{j-1}}\phi w\, d\mu
	\qquad\text{for every }j\geq0 .
\end{equation}
Indeed, since $A^j\subset U^j$ and $\mathcal F^j$ is disjoint,
CZ\ref{itemCZU} gives
$$\int_{A^{j}}(\phi w)^p\, d\mu
\leq\sum_{P\in\mathcal F^j}\mu(P)\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_{P}(\phi w)^p\, d\mu
\leq C_\mu\alpha_j^{p}\,\mu(U^j),$$
and Lemma \ref{lemBesicovitchSelection} applied to $\mathcal F^j$, followed by
{(\ref{eqGehringStep1})}, yields
$$\begin{aligned}
\mu(U^j)&\leq{\widetilde{C}}_\mu\sum_{P\in\mathcal G}\mu(3P)
<\frac{2C_{RH}C_\mu^{3}{\widetilde{C}}_\mu}{\alpha_j}
\sum_{P\in\mathcal G}\int_{3P\cap A^{j-1}}\phi w\, d\mu\\
&\leq\frac{2C_{\mathrm B}C_{RH}C_\mu^{3}{\widetilde{C}}_\mu}{\alpha_j}
\int_{A^{j-1}}\phi w\, d\mu .
\end{aligned}$$
Adding
$\int_{A^{j-1}\setminus A^{j}}(\phi w)^p\, d\mu
\leq\alpha_j^{p-1}\int_{A^{j-1}\setminus A^{j}}\phi w\, d\mu$ gives
{(\ref{eqGehringSecondStep})}.

\emph{Step 3.} Fix
\begin{equation}\label{eqTauChoiceGehring}
	\tau\coloneqq \min\left\{\frac{C_\mu^{2}}{(4C_1)^{\frac1{p-1}}},\
	\frac1{2C_{RH}C_\mu}\right\},
\end{equation}
so that {(\ref{eqTauSmallGehring})} holds and $\rho^{\,p-1}\geq4C_1>2C_1$. Then
{(\ref{eqGehringSecondStep})} is hypothesis {(\ref{eqBasicLevels})} of Lemma
\ref{lemLevelToQ} for $\mu|_V$, $f=\phi w$ and this $p$, so for every
$p<q\leq q_0$, with $q_0$ as in {(\ref{eqQZero})},
$$\int_{V}(\phi w)^q\, d\mu
\leq C_{C_1,\rho,p,q}\,\alpha_0^{\,q-p}\int_{V}(\phi w)^p\, d\mu
\leq C_{C_1,\rho,p,q}\,\mu(V)^{q+1}
\left(\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_{V}w^{p}\, d\mu\right)^{\frac qp},$$
where we used $\phi\leq\mu(V)$ and the definition of $\alpha_0$. Since
$w\leq\phi w/\inf_K\phi$ on $K$,
$$\begin{aligned}
\left(\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_{K}w^q\, d\mu\right)^{\frac1q}
&\leq\frac1{\mu(K)^{\frac1q}\inf_{K}\phi}
\left(\int_{V}(\phi w)^q\, d\mu\right)^{\frac1q}\\
&\leq\frac{C_{C_1,\rho,p,q}^{\frac1q}\,\mu(V)^{1+\frac1q}}
{\mu(K)^{\frac1q}\,\inf_{K}\phi}
\left(\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_{V}w^{p}\, d\mu\right)^{\frac1p} ,
\end{aligned}$$
and Lemma \ref{lemInfPhi} bounds $\inf_K\phi$ from below, giving the stated
inequality.
<!-- end proof-25 -->
<!-- proof-26: body_md -->
Let $Q\in{\mathcal Q}_V$, so that $3Q\subset V$. Cover $Q$ by restricted balls
$\{\Delta_i\}_{i\leq N}$ centred at points of $Q$ and of radius
$c\,\ell(Q)/\gamma$, with $c=c(A_0)$ chosen so that
$\gamma\Delta_i\subset3Q$; since ${\operatorname{diam}} Q\leq A_0\ell(Q)$, we may take
$N\leq C(d)\gamma^{d}$. Each $\gamma\Delta_i$ has radius comparable to
$\ell(Q)$, so $\mu(\gamma\Delta_i)\geq C_\mu^{-m_d}\mu(Q)$ with $m_d$
dimensional, and $\mu(\Delta_i)\leq\mu(3Q)\leq C_\mu\mu(Q)$. Hence, using
$\left(\sum_i a_i\right)^{1/p}\leq\sum_i a_i^{1/p}$,
$$\begin{aligned}
\left(\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_{Q}w^p\, d\mu\right)^{\frac1p}
&\leq\sum_{i}\left(\frac{\mu(\Delta_i)}{\mu(Q)}
\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_{\Delta_i}w^p\, d\mu\right)^{\frac1p}
\leq C_\mu C_{RH}\sum_{i}\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_{\gamma\Delta_i}w\, d\mu\\
&\leq C(d,C_\mu)\,\gamma^{d}C_{RH}\,\frac{\nu(3Q)}{\mu(Q)} ,
\end{aligned}$$
because $\gamma\Delta_i\subset3Q$. Thus $\nu\in wRH_p(\mu,{\mathcal Q}_V)$ with a
constant
$$C'_{RH}\leq C(d,C_\mu)\,\gamma^{d}C_{RH},$$
and Lemma \ref{lemGehringEnlarged} applies with $C'_{RH}$ in place of
$C_{RH}$. Since $\gamma$ enters only through $C'_{RH}$, and $C'_{RH}$ only
through the product $\gamma^{d}C_{RH}$, the exponent ${\varepsilon}_0=q_0-p$ depends on
$\gamma$ and $C_{RH}$ only through that product.
<!-- end proof-26 -->
<!-- theorem-4: body_md -->
\label{theoGehringWeak}
Let $\mu$ be a doubling Radon measure in ${\mathbb R}^d$ and let $\nu\in wRH_p(\mu)$
with $1<p<\infty$. Then there is ${\varepsilon}_0>0$, depending on $d$, $C_\mu$, $p$ and
$C_{RH}$, such that $\nu\in wRH_{p+{\varepsilon}}(\mu)$ for every $0<{\varepsilon}<{\varepsilon}_0$.
<!-- end theorem-4 -->
<!-- proof-27: body_md -->
Let $\Delta$ be a restricted ball. Since
$\frac{\nu(2\Delta)}{\mu(\Delta)}\leq C_\mu\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_{2\Delta}w\, d\mu$, the
hypothesis of Theorem \ref{theoCintro} holds with $\gamma=2$ and constant
$C_\mu C_{RH}$; apply it with $V=\frac32\Delta$ and $K=\overline\Delta$. As
$\operatorname{dist}(K,X\setminus V)\geq\frac{r(\Delta)}2$, ${\operatorname{diam}} K\leq2r(\Delta)$ and
$\mu(V)\leq C_\mu\mu(K)$, the resulting constants do not depend on $\Delta$,
and for every ${\varepsilon}<{\varepsilon}_0$
$$\left(\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_{\Delta}w^{p+{\varepsilon}}\, d\mu\right)^{\frac1{p+{\varepsilon}}}
\leq C\left(\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_{\frac32\Delta}w^{p}\, d\mu\right)^{\frac1p} .$$
It remains to undo the enlargement. Cover $\frac32\Delta$ by restricted balls
$\{\Delta_i\}_{i\leq N}$ centred in $\frac32\Delta$ of radius
$\frac{r(\Delta)}8$, so that $2\Delta_i\subset2\Delta$, with $N$ dimensional
and $\mu(\Delta_i)\geq C_\mu^{-m_d}\mu(\Delta)$. Then {(\ref{eqWeakRH})} gives
$$\int_{\frac32\Delta}w^p\, d\mu
\leq\sum_{i}\mu(\Delta_i)\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_{\Delta_i}w^p\, d\mu
\leq C_{RH}^{p}\sum_{i}\frac{\nu(2\Delta_i)^{p}}{\mu(\Delta_i)^{p-1}}
\leq C\,\frac{\nu(2\Delta)^{p}}{\mu(\Delta)^{p-1}} ,$$
so $\left(\;\mathchoice
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\displaystyle{\textstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
{{\setbox0=\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\scriptscriptstyle-}{\int}$ }
\vcenter{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-.58\wd0}} 
\!\int_{\frac32\Delta}w^p\, d\mu\right)^{1/p}
\leq C\frac{\nu(2\Delta)}{\mu(\Delta)}$ and the theorem follows.
<!-- end proof-27 -->
<!-- remark-15: body_md -->
\label{remGehringStrong}
The dilation cannot be dispensed with, and the dyadic and ball settings part
company here. On the basis ${\mathcal D}\cup3{\mathcal D}$ there is a \emph{strong} Gehring
lemma: if $\nu\in RH_p(\mu,{\mathcal B}_{Q_0})$ with $1<p<\infty$ then
$\nu\in RH_{p+{\varepsilon}}(\mu,{\mathcal B}_{Q_0})$ for ${\varepsilon}$ small, which is
\cite[Thm.~3.1]{AndersonWeirich}; one may also read it off from the proof of
Lemma \ref{lemGehringEnlarged} by taking ${\mathcal W}=\{Q_0\}$ and replacing $3P$ by
$P$ throughout. For balls in a general space of homogeneous type the
corresponding statement is false, by \cite[\S7.3]{AndersonHytonenTapiola};
for restricted Euclidean balls we do not know whether it holds; see \S\ref{secOpen}. For
metric measure spaces enjoying an annular decay property the dilation can be
removed, by \cite[Cor.~3.2]{Maasalo}.
<!-- end remark-15 -->
